## Housing

_Across both eras housing has been the most heavily promised policy area in New Zealand politics: Labour campaigned on building its way out of the crisis and tightening rules on landlords and investors, while the 2023 coalition campaigned on reversing those settings, freeing up land and emptying emergency housing motels._

### Build 5,000 new public and community homes a year, lift Kāinga Ora's borrowing cap from $7.1 billion to $12 billion, and clear the social housing waiting list within five years.

**Greens** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Not delivered**  
`housing-greens-homes-for-all`

> 5,000 homes a year and waiting list cleared by 2025 -> approx 2,000 a year, register at 25,483 in late 2023

None of the three parts was achieved: the annual build rate, the borrowing cap change, or the waiting-list target. The Greens did not hold the housing portfolio in Cabinet.

- Promise (manifesto): [interest.co.nz](https://www.interest.co.nz/property/106435/greens-borrow-5-billion-more-build-5000-homes-year-and-clear-social-housing-list) - Green Party 2020 'Homes for All' housing policy, released 9 August 2020
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/labour-delivers-12000-more-public-homes)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/more-social-homes-better-targeted)

### Develop a rent-to-own scheme or similar progressive home ownership models as part of the KiwiBuild programme, with a set allocation of KiwiBuild homes.

**Greens** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`housing-greens-rent-to-own`

Progressive home ownership products were created, but not as a defined allocation within KiwiBuild, and at modest scale.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [interest.co.nz](https://www.interest.co.nz/news/90522/greens-outline-confidence-and-supply-agreement-new-government-which-includes-20-policy) - Labour-Green Party Confidence and Supply Agreement, October 2017
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KiwiBuild)
- Evidence: [thespinoff.co.nz](https://thespinoff.co.nz/politics/12-10-2023/election-2023-the-housing-policies-in-two-minutes)

### Take action on homelessness through the Aotearoa New Zealand Homelessness Action Plan, a cross-agency plan backed by over $300 million. It committed to 1,000 additional transitional housing places and 18 immediate actions.

**Greens** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Largely delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`gapfill-greens-homelessness-action-plan`

> 1,000 additional transitional housing places by end 2020 -> 1,005 delivered as at February 2021

The named plan and its numeric transitional housing target were delivered on time. But homelessness, as measured by emergency housing and register numbers, rose across the term.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [scoop.co.nz](https://www.scoop.co.nz/stories/PA2010/S00117/cooperation-agreement-between-the-nz-labour-party-and-the-green-party-of-aotearoa-nz.htm) - Labour-Greens Cooperation Agreement 2020, action on homelessness, with Marama Davidson as Associate Minister of Housing (Homelessness)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/government-steps-action-prevent-homelessness)
- Evidence: [scoop.co.nz](https://www.scoop.co.nz/stories/PA2105/S00131/report-shows-progress-on-homelessness-action-plan.htm)
- Evidence: [hud.govt.nz](https://www.hud.govt.nz/our-work/aotearoa-new-zealand-homelessness-action-plan-2020-2023)

### Build 100,000 affordable homes over 10 years through KiwiBuild, including 1,000 in the first year.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Not delivered**  
`housing-labour-kiwibuild`

> 100,000 homes over 10 years -> approx 2,229 completed by Dec 2023

The promising party formally abandoned the headline target in 2019, and delivery reached roughly 2 per cent of the promised number by the end of the term.

- Promise (manifesto): [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KiwiBuild) - Labour 2017 manifesto / KiwiBuild campaign policy
- Evidence: [nzherald.co.nz](https://www.nzherald.co.nz/nz/kiwibuild-reset-government-axes-its-100000-homes-over-10-years-target/AP2SYCESGXEEE6A3GWOOSDWM7Y/)
- Evidence: [stuff.co.nz](https://www.stuff.co.nz/national/politics/300347258/kiwibuild-reaches-first-target--two-years-late-15000-homes-behind-schedule)

### Ban non-resident overseas buyers from buying existing residential homes in New Zealand.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered**  
`housing-labour-foreign-buyer-ban`

> Ban promised -> in force 22 Oct 2018 (Australia and Singapore exempt)

Legislated and in force within the first year of the term, substantially as promised, with the trade-treaty exemptions that were flagged at the time.

- Promise (manifesto): [cnbc.com](https://www.cnbc.com/2017/10/25/new-zealand-property-ban-to-take-effect-on-foreign-buyers.html) - Labour 2017 campaign commitment to ban non-resident foreign buyers of existing homes
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overseas_Investment_Amendment_Act_2018)

### Require every rental home to be warm and dry, by setting enforceable minimum standards for heating, insulation, ventilation, moisture and draught stopping.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Largely delivered**  
`housing-labour-healthy-homes`

> All rentals compliant by 1 July 2024 -> final private-rental deadline moved to 1 July 2025

The legislation and standards were delivered early in the term. But the deadline for full private-rental compliance was extended, and fell after the promising government left office.

- Promise (manifesto): [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Healthy_Homes_Guarantee_Act_2017) - Labour 2017 manifesto / Healthy Homes Guarantee Bill
- Evidence: [hud.govt.nz](https://www.hud.govt.nz/our-work/healthy-homes-standards)
- Evidence: [tenancy.govt.nz](https://www.tenancy.govt.nz/about-tenancy-services/news/healthy-homes-standards-final-deadline-1-july-2025/)

### Deliver 18,000 additional public and transitional housing places by 2024.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Largely delivered**  
`housing-labour-public-homes-18000`

> 18,000 public and transitional places by 2024 -> 12,017 net additional public homes plus 4,000+ transitional places by June 2023

The build programme was the largest since the 1970s, and the combined public plus transitional total was close to the 18,000 places promised. But it did not arrest growth in the waiting list.

- Promise (manifesto): [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sixth_Labour_Government_of_New_Zealand) - Labour 2020 election platform, public housing build programme
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/labour-delivers-12000-more-public-homes)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/more-social-homes-better-targeted)

### Reform the Residential Tenancies Act to improve security of tenure for renters, including removing no-cause terminations and limiting rent increases.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered**  
`housing-labour-tenancy-security`

> No-cause terminations removed and rent rises limited to once a year -> both enacted in 2020

The promised statutory changes were enacted and in force for the rest of the term. A different government reversed them later.

- Promise (manifesto): [thespinoff.co.nz](https://thespinoff.co.nz/politics/12-10-2023/election-2023-the-housing-policies-in-two-minutes) - Labour renters policy (2017 and 2020 terms), restated in Labour's 2023 housing platform
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sixth_Labour_Government_of_New_Zealand)
- Evidence: [hud.govt.nz](https://www.hud.govt.nz/our-work/residential-tenancies-amendment-act-2024)

### Strengthen the Overseas Investment Act and create a comprehensive register of foreign-owned land and housing.

**NZ First** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Largely delivered**  
`housing-nzfirst-overseas-investment-register`

> Two commitments -> ban delivered Oct 2018, comprehensive foreign ownership register not established

The main limb, strengthening the Overseas Investment Act, was delivered in full early in the term. The separate comprehensive register was not created as described.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [nzherald.co.nz](https://www.nzherald.co.nz/nz/labour-nz-first-coalition-at-a-glance/OGW5YCYCI4D4AHS6YIS64UKZNA/) - Labour-NZ First Coalition Agreement, 24 October 2017
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overseas_Investment_Amendment_Act_2018)

### Abolish the First Home Grant and the progressive home ownership scheme.

**ACT** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`housing-act-first-home-grant-abolish`

> First Home Grant abolished -> closed to new applicants 22 May 2024

The First Home Grant, the central element of the policy, was abolished in the Government's first Budget.

- Promise (manifesto): [thespinoff.co.nz](https://thespinoff.co.nz/politics/12-10-2023/election-2023-the-housing-policies-in-two-minutes) - ACT 2023 election housing policy
- Evidence: [1news.co.nz](https://www.1news.co.nz/2024/05/22/budget-2024-first-home-grant-cut-1500-social-homes-in-its-place/)
- Evidence: [interest.co.nz](https://www.interest.co.nz/property/127882/k%C4%81inga-ora-will-not-accept-any-new-applications-first-home-grant-which-will-be)

### Allow landlords to charge a pet bond, so that more rentals will accept pets.

**ACT** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`housing-act-pet-bonds`

> Pet bond allowed -> up to 2 weeks' rent from 1 Dec 2025

Legislated as promised, in force from 1 December 2025.

- Promise (manifesto): [thespinoff.co.nz](https://thespinoff.co.nz/politics/12-10-2023/election-2023-the-housing-policies-in-two-minutes) - ACT 2023 election housing policy (adopted in the National-ACT coalition agreement)
- Evidence: [hud.govt.nz](https://www.hud.govt.nz/our-work/residential-tenancies-amendment-act-2024)
- Evidence: [nzherald.co.nz](https://www.nzherald.co.nz/nz/new-government-coalition-details-at-a-glance-what-you-need-to-know/FBTX3E6HXREFXDTOA6AA5K7IUQ/)

### Eliminate the bright-line test on residential property entirely.

**ACT** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Not delivered**  
`housing-act-abolish-brightline`

> Bright-line abolished -> reduced to 2 years, still in force at Aug 2026

The test was shortened rather than eliminated, so ACT's specific promise did not happen.

- Promise (manifesto): [thespinoff.co.nz](https://thespinoff.co.nz/politics/12-10-2023/election-2023-the-housing-policies-in-two-minutes) - ACT 2023 election housing and tax policy
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/first-steps-tax-and-income-relief-announced)

### Oppose and unwind the National Policy Statement on Urban Development and the Medium Density Residential Standards.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`housing-nzfirst-density-rules`

> Remove NPS-UD and MDRS -> MDRS made optional for Auckland and Christchurch; NPS-UD retained

The MDRS limb was largely advanced. But the NPS-UD was kept rather than removed, and intensification obligations on councils were extended.

- Promise (manifesto): [thespinoff.co.nz](https://thespinoff.co.nz/politics/12-10-2023/election-2023-the-housing-policies-in-two-minutes) - NZ First 2023 election housing policy
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/going-housing-growth-stage-one-unveiled)
- Evidence: [hud.govt.nz](https://www.hud.govt.nz/our-work/going-for-housing-growth-programme)

### Restore full interest deductibility for residential rental property owners, reversing Labour's 2021 interest limitation rules.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`housing-national-interest-deductibility`

> Full deductibility restored -> 80% from 1 Apr 2024, 100% from 1 Apr 2025

Full deductibility was restored on the announced timetable within the term.

- Promise (manifesto): [thespinoff.co.nz](https://thespinoff.co.nz/politics/12-10-2023/election-2023-the-housing-policies-in-two-minutes) - National 2023 election housing and tax policy
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/government-agrees-restore-interest-deductions)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/first-steps-tax-and-income-relief-announced)

### Reduce the bright-line test on residential property from 10 years back to two years.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`housing-national-brightline-two-years`

> 10 years -> 2 years from 1 July 2024

Delivered in full and on the date announced.

- Promise (manifesto): [thespinoff.co.nz](https://thespinoff.co.nz/politics/12-10-2023/election-2023-the-housing-policies-in-two-minutes) - National 2023 election housing and tax policy
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/first-steps-tax-and-income-relief-announced)

### Bring back 90-day no-cause terminations for periodic tenancies, and remove the automatic rollover of fixed-term tenancies.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`housing-national-no-cause-terminations`

> 90-day no-cause notice restored -> in force 30 Jan 2025

Both limbs were legislated and in force within about 14 months of taking office.

- Promise (manifesto): [thespinoff.co.nz](https://thespinoff.co.nz/politics/12-10-2023/election-2023-the-housing-policies-in-two-minutes) - National 2023 election housing policy
- Evidence: [hud.govt.nz](https://www.hud.govt.nz/our-work/residential-tenancies-amendment-act-2024)

### Allow foreign buyers to buy homes worth more than $2 million, taxed at 15 per cent, raising around $700 million a year to help fund tax cuts.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Superseded**  
`housing-national-foreign-buyer-tax`

> $2m threshold with 15% tax and approx $700m a year -> dropped; $5m threshold for investor-visa holders from early 2026, no 15% tax

The manifesto policy was abandoned in coalition negotiation before the Government took office. The later $5 million investor-visa change is a materially narrower policy.

- Promise (manifesto): [nzherald.co.nz](https://www.nzherald.co.nz/nz/politics/election-2023-national-tax-cuts-luxon-unveils-146b-plan-foreign-homebuyers-to-foot-bill/IMWYSQWTB5ELVFJLRNSJL4XTR4/) - National 2023 tax plan, announced 30 August 2023
- Evidence: [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/news/political/582054/what-changes-to-new-zealand-s-foreign-buyers-real-estate-ban-will-mean)
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overseas_Investment_Amendment_Act_2018)

### Retain the First Home Grant for first-home buyers (in contrast to ACT, which campaigned to abolish it).

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Abandoned**  
`housing-national-first-home-grant`

> First Home Grant retained -> abolished 22 May 2024; $245m of savings, $140m redirected to approx 1,500 social homes

The scheme National said it would keep was scrapped by National in its first Budget, six months into the term.

- Promise (manifesto): [thespinoff.co.nz](https://thespinoff.co.nz/politics/12-10-2023/election-2023-the-housing-policies-in-two-minutes) - National 2023 election housing policy (support for retaining the First Home Grant)
- Evidence: [1news.co.nz](https://www.1news.co.nz/2024/05/22/budget-2024-first-home-grant-cut-1500-social-homes-in-its-place/)
- Evidence: [interest.co.nz](https://www.interest.co.nz/property/127882/k%C4%81inga-ora-will-not-accept-any-new-applications-first-home-grant-which-will-be)

### Allow councils to opt out of the mandatory Medium Density Residential Standards, while requiring them to zone for housing growth.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`housing-national-mdrs-optional`

> MDRS optional for all councils -> opt-out enacted for Auckland and Christchurch (Aug 2025); wider changes still in progress

Auckland and Christchurch were enabled to opt out, and the policy framework is set. But the general opt-out and the 30-year zoning obligations were not yet law for most councils at August 2026, and the term is unfinished.

- Promise (manifesto): [thespinoff.co.nz](https://thespinoff.co.nz/politics/12-10-2023/election-2023-the-housing-policies-in-two-minutes) - National 2023 housing policy and National-ACT coalition agreement (flexibility of the Medium Density Residential Standards)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/going-housing-growth-stage-one-unveiled)
- Evidence: [hud.govt.nz](https://www.hud.govt.nz/our-work/going-for-housing-growth-programme)

### Reduce the number of households living in emergency housing motels by 75 per cent by 2030.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`housing-national-emergency-housing-target`

> 75% reduction by 2030 -> 3,141 households (Dec 2023) to 591 (Dec 2024) and 471 (May 2026)

The stated numeric target was met, and exceeded, five years ahead of the deadline.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/emergency-housing-target-achieved-five-years-early) - Coalition Government Targets, announced 2024
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/emergency-housing-target-achieved-five-years-early)
- Evidence: [newswire.co.nz](https://newswire.co.nz/2026/06/labour-scrap-emergency-housing-targets/)
- Evidence: [scoop.co.nz](https://www.scoop.co.nz/stories/PO2608/S00046/performance-targets-linked-to-reducing-emergency-housing-numbers-dropped.htm)

### Conduct an independent review of Kāinga Ora's financial situation, procurement and asset management, and act on its findings.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`housing-national-kainga-ora-review`

> Independent review promised -> delivered May 2024; peak debt forecast cut from approx $29b to $19.5b by 2029/30

The review was commissioned, completed, published and acted on within the term, with a published turnaround plan and reported results against it.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [hud.govt.nz](https://www.hud.govt.nz/news/government-releases-the-independent-review-of-kainga-ora) - Coalition Government commitment to an independent review of Kāinga Ora, announced December 2023
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/turnaround-plan-get-k%C4%81inga-ora-back-track)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/k%C4%81inga-ora%E2%80%99s-turnaround-plan-working)

### Allow a granny flat of up to 70 square metres to be built without a building consent or a resource consent.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`housing-national-granny-flats`

> 70 sqm granny flats consent-free -> in force January 2026

Both the building and resource consent exemptions were legislated and in force, at the promised 70 square metre size.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [mbie.govt.nz](https://www.mbie.govt.nz/about/news/granny-flats-exemption-in-place-from-early-2026) - Coalition Government policy programme, granny flats consent exemption (announced 2024)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/updated-granny-flat-rules-increase-choice)
- Evidence: [mbie.govt.nz](https://www.mbie.govt.nz/about/news/granny-flats-exemption-in-place-from-early-2026)

## Cost of Living & Tax

_Both governments campaigned on making households better off. Labour through targeted transfers and a higher top tax rate while ruling out a capital gains tax, National through income tax threshold relief and a childcare rebate funded by measures that were partly abandoned in coalition talks._

### Set up a Tax Working Group in the first term to design a fairer tax system, including consideration of a capital gains tax. Take its recommendations to voters at the 2020 election.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Abandoned**  
`cost-of-living-labour-tax-working-group-cgt`

> Tax Working Group's central recommendation (broad capital gains tax) -> ruled out April 2019, none legislated

The working group was set up and reported as promised. The government then dropped its central recommendation, and Labour later ruled the policy out entirely.

- Promise (manifesto): [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/news/political/532793/capital-gains-tax-a-timeline-of-politicians-ruling-it-in-and-out) - Labour 2017 manifesto / 2017 campaign tax policy
- Evidence: [scoop.co.nz](https://www.scoop.co.nz/stories/PO1905/S00214/nz-labour-government-rules-out-capital-gains-tax.htm)
- Evidence: [nzherald.co.nz](https://www.nzherald.co.nz/nz/pm-jacinda-ardern-has-ruled-out-implementing-a-capital-gains-tax-while-she-is-at-the-helm-of-labour/IQ4FD7CLYKKLU6YAH2H2S4MDSE/)
- Evidence: [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/programmes/news-extras/story/2018902030/election-2023-rnz-s-guide-to-party-policy)

### Introduce a new top personal income tax rate of 39 percent on income above $180,000, affecting roughly the top 2 percent of earners.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered**  
`cost-of-living-labour-39-percent-top-rate`

> 39% on income over $180,000 promised -> legislated December 2020, in force 1 April 2021

Legislated within four months of the election at the promised rate, threshold and start date.

- Promise (manifesto): [interest.co.nz](https://www.interest.co.nz/news/106960/labour-campaigns-introducing-new-top-income-tax-rate-39-income-over-180k-rules-out) - Labour 2020 manifesto / 2020 campaign tax policy
- Evidence: [taxpolicy.ird.govt.nz](https://www.taxpolicy.ird.govt.nz/news/2020/2020-12-02-introduction-taxation-income-tax-rate-other-amendments-bill)
- Evidence: [nzherald.co.nz](https://www.nzherald.co.nz/nz/politics/labours-new-39-per-cent-top-tax-rate-nets-44000-extra-taxpayers/YEPQKEZDFOIQI4KPRJUYLXKV7I/)

### The 39 percent top rate would be the only tax change in the 2020-23 term: no wealth tax, no capital gains tax and no change to GST.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Partly delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`cost-of-living-labour-no-other-new-taxes`

> 1 promised new tax (39% rate) -> 39% rate plus removal of landlord interest deductibility, a 10-year bright-line test and GST on online platforms

The specific taxes Labour ruled out were not introduced. Two large revenue-raising changes outside the stated plan were legislated during the term.

- Promise (manifesto): [interest.co.nz](https://www.interest.co.nz/news/106960/labour-campaigns-introducing-new-top-income-tax-rate-39-income-over-180k-rules-out) - Labour 2020 manifesto / 2020 campaign tax policy
- Evidence: [assets.kpmg.com](https://assets.kpmg.com/content/dam/kpmg/nz/pdf/2023/09/2023-election-tax-policy-v4.pdf)
- Evidence: [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/news/political/485228/national-claims-airbnb-and-uber-price-hikes-possible-under-govt-s-planned-tax-changes)
- Evidence: [bakertillysr.nz](https://bakertillysr.nz/news/tax-talk-heres-how-the-new-app-tax-regulations-will-apply/)

### Cancel National's legislated April 2018 income tax threshold cuts and replace them with a Families Package worth $890 million in 2018/19. The package includes a Winter Energy Payment of $450 a year for single superannuitants and beneficiaries and $700 for couples, a Best Start payment of $60 a week per child, a higher Family Tax Credit and an increased Working for Families abatement threshold of $42,700.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered**  
`cost-of-living-labour-families-package`

> Winter Energy Payment $450 single / $700 couple and Best Start $60 a week promised -> both legislated December 2017 and paid from 2018

Legislated within the first three months of the term at the promised payment rates and thresholds.

- Promise (manifesto): [img.scoop.co.nz](https://img.scoop.co.nz/media/pdfs/1707/Families_Package_factsheet.pdf) - Labour 2017 manifesto / Families Package campaign policy
- Evidence: [treasury.govt.nz](https://www.treasury.govt.nz/publications/information-release/families-package-information-release)
- Evidence: [msd.govt.nz](https://www.msd.govt.nz/about-msd-and-our-work/newsroom/2017/families-package-qas.html)

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Not delivered**  
`cost-of-living-labour-gst-fruit-and-vegetables`

> GST off fresh and frozen fruit and vegetables from 1 April 2024 -> not legislated; GST still applies to all food in August 2026

GST was not removed from fruit and vegetables. Labour left office three weeks after making the promise and never had the numbers to legislate it.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered**

Both market studies were carried out as promised. The grocery study was followed by a regulator and two Acts before the end of the term.

### Move to a flatter, lower income tax scale: 17.5 percent under $60,000, 30 percent from $60,000 to $70,000, 33 percent from $70,000 to $180,000 and 39 percent above that. Extend the 30 percent bracket to $180,000 in 2025/26, and cut the top rate from 39 percent to 33 percent in 2026/27. Pay a carbon tax refund of about $243 per person in 2023/24.

**ACT** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Superseded**  
`cost-of-living-act-tax-cuts`

> Top rate cut from 39% to 33% by 2026/27 and a $243 per person carbon tax refund -> neither delivered; 39% rate still in force in August 2026

ACT's rate structure and carbon dividend were both dropped in favour of National's tax plan in the coalition agreement, and none of the promised rate changes were legislated.

- Promise (manifesto): [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/news/political/498449/watch-act-leader-david-seymour-reveals-alternative-budget) - ACT 2023 alternative budget and tax policy

- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_New_Zealand_mini-budget)

### Make the lowest income tax bracket (the first $14,000 of income) tax free no later than 1 April 2027, worth $28.27 a week to workers. Adjust tax brackets for inflation, with the first adjustment in 2027 and reviews every three years.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Not delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`cost-of-living-nzfirst-tax-free-threshold`

> $14,000 tax-free bracket worth $28.27 a week by 1 April 2027 -> not legislated as at August 2026

As at August 2026 no tax-free bracket or bracket indexation regime has been legislated, and the policy was not picked up in the coalition agreement.

### Give SuperGold cardholders a 50 percent local authority rates rebate of up to $1,600 a year, costed in the manifesto at about $480 million for couples.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Not delivered**

### From 1 July 2024, lift income tax thresholds to compensate for inflation: the 10.5 percent bracket from $14,000 to $15,600, the 17.5 percent bracket from $48,000 to $53,500 and the 30 percent bracket from $70,000 to $78,100. The cost is about $8.9 billion over four years.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`cost-of-living-national-tax-thresholds`

> Thresholds $15,600 / $53,500 / $78,100 from 1 July 2024 -> legislated in Budget 2024, effective 31 July 2024

The promised bracket thresholds were legislated in the first Budget of the term, four weeks later than the promised start date.

### Deliver up to $250 a fortnight in extra income to an average-income household with children (household income around $120,000). This would come from threshold changes, FamilyBoost and Working for Families increases together.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Partly delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`cost-of-living-national-250-a-fortnight`

> Up to $250 a fortnight for an average family -> $78 a fortnight average for households with children; about 3,000 households qualified for the full amount

Real tax relief was delivered. The typical gain for households with children was under a third of the headline figure, and only a very small number of households received the advertised maximum.

### Introduce FamilyBoost, a childcare tax rebate of up to $150 a fortnight (25 percent of early childhood fees). It would abate from a household income of $140,000 and cut out at $180,000, benefiting an extra 130,000 households at a cost of $996 million over four years.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`cost-of-living-national-familyboost`

> Up to $150 a fortnight for an extra 130,000 households -> about 60,000 families claiming by mid-2025, 249 receiving the full amount

The rebate exists and pays at the promised headline rate. Take-up fell far short of the 130,000 households promised, and almost no families received the advertised maximum until the scheme was redesigned a year in.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Superseded**

## Economy, Jobs & Fiscal

_Labour campaigned on tight fiscal rules, sector-wide bargaining and a Reserve Bank mandate that included employment, then spent through a pandemic; National campaigned on reversing those settings and returning to surplus by 2026/27, and by August 2026 was forecasting its first surplus two years later than promised with unemployment at a decade high._

### Sign up to and govern by Labour's Budget Responsibility Rules. These include reducing net core Crown debt to less than 20 percent of GDP within five years and keeping government spending at or below 30 percent of GDP.

**Greens** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Abandoned**  
`economy-greens-budget-responsibility-rules`

> Debt below 20% of GDP and spending at or below 30% of GDP -> rules formally scrapped by the Greens in December 2019

The Greens explicitly and publicly dropped a fiscal commitment they had campaigned on, part-way through the term.

- Promise (manifesto): [pressreader.com](https://www.pressreader.com/new-zealand/the-timaru-herald/20191211/281676846790667) - Green Party 2017 election commitment (Budget Responsibility Rules co-signed with Labour, March 2017)
- Evidence: [pressreader.com](https://www.pressreader.com/new-zealand/the-timaru-herald/20191211/281676846790667)

### Stimulate up to $1 billion of investment in low carbon industries by 2020, through a proposed Green Investment Fund.

**Greens** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Partly delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`gapfill-greens-green-investment-finance`

> Up to $1b of low carbon investment stimulated by 2020 -> NZGIF capitalised at $100m in 2019, about half committed by 2020; capital reached $700m by May 2023

The institution was created and its capital grew, but the $1 billion by 2020 figure in the agreement was not close to being met on that date.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [thestandard.org.nz](https://thestandard.org.nz/labour-green-confidence-and-supply-agreement-released/) - Labour-Green Confidence and Supply Agreement 2017, policy goal 4, stimulate up to $1 billion of investment in low carbon industries by 2020
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/100-million-investment-fund-launched-invest-reducing-emissions)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/pioneering-green-investor-gets-significant-boost)
- Evidence: [treasury.govt.nz](https://www.treasury.govt.nz/sites/default/files/2018-12/establishing-nz-green-investment-finance-limited.pdf)

### Introduce a wealth tax on net assets over $1 million, new top personal income tax rates, and a Guaranteed Minimum Income of at least $325 a week for everyone not in full-time paid work.

**Greens** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Superseded**  
`gapfill-greens-wealth-tax-gmi`

> Wealth tax over $1m net assets and a $325 a week Guaranteed Minimum Income -> neither included in the 2020 Cooperation Agreement

The party's flagship 2020 policy was excluded from the cooperation agreement negotiated after the election and never became government policy.

- Promise (manifesto): [interest.co.nz](https://www.interest.co.nz/news/105728/greens-propose-introducing-universal-payment-least-325week-those-who-dont-work-full) - Green Party 2020 Poverty Action Plan, wealth tax, new top income tax rates and a Guaranteed Minimum Income
- Evidence: [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/news/politics/428348/what-else-apart-from-a-wealth-tax-the-shape-of-a-labour-greens-coalition)
- Evidence: [scoop.co.nz](https://www.scoop.co.nz/stories/PA2010/S00117/cooperation-agreement-between-the-nz-labour-party-and-the-green-party-of-aotearoa-nz.htm)

### Meet five Budget Responsibility Rules. The strictest is to reduce net core Crown debt to below 20 percent of GDP within five years of taking office, and to keep core Crown spending at or around 30 percent of GDP, while running sustainable operating surpluses across the economic cycle.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Partly delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`economy-labour-budget-responsibility-rules`

> Net core Crown debt below 20% of GDP within 5 years -> on track pre-COVID, target dropped in 2020; operating deficits every year from 2019/20 to 2022/23

The rules were met in the first term and abandoned in the second. The debt and surplus targets were not in place at the end of the era.

- Promise (manifesto): [pressreader.com](https://www.pressreader.com/new-zealand/the-timaru-herald/20191211/281676846790667) - Labour 2017 fiscal plan / Budget Responsibility Rules (agreed with the Greens, March 2017)
- Evidence: [treasury.govt.nz](https://www.treasury.govt.nz/publications/efu/budget-economic-and-fiscal-update-2026)
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sixth_Labour_Government_of_New_Zealand)

### Introduce Fair Pay Agreements, allowing unions and employers to bargain sector-wide minimum terms and conditions binding on all employers in an occupation or industry.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Largely delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`economy-labour-fair-pay-agreements`

> Fair Pay Agreements promised in 2017 -> Act passed October 2022; 0 agreements concluded before repeal in December 2023

The system was legislated as promised, but only in the final year of the second term, and no agreement was ever concluded under it.

- Promise (manifesto): [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fair_Pay_Agreements_Act_2022) - Labour 2017 and 2020 manifestos / workplace relations policy

### Raise the adult minimum wage from $15.75 to $20 an hour by 2021, starting with $16.50 from 1 April 2018. Workplace Relations Minister Iain Lees-Galloway said on 22 December 2017: 'we are committed to increasing the minimum wage to $20 by 2021'.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered**  
`economy-labour-minimum-wage-20`

> $15.75 in 2017 -> $20.00 on 1 April 2021 as promised, and $22.70 by April 2023

The exact rate was reached on the exact promised date.

### Abolish 90-day trial periods. They allow an employer to dismiss a new employee within their first 90 days, without the employee being able to bring a personal grievance for unjustified dismissal.

Trial periods were narrowed rather than abolished, and the narrowing lasted less than five years.

- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sixth_National_Government_of_New_Zealand)

Legislated within the first year of the term, and still in force in August 2026.

### Establish a Regional Development (Provincial Growth) Fund of $1 billion a year to invest in the regions.

**NZ First** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Largely delivered**  
`economy-nzfirst-provincial-growth-fund`

**ACT** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Not delivered**  
`economy-act-public-sector-back-to-2017-levels`

> Public sector back to 2017 levels -> 64,535 public service FTEs at March 2026, versus a December 2023 peak of 65,699 and a June 2017 base roughly a third smaller

Public service headcount at March 2026 was only about 1,200 below its December 2023 peak and remained far above 2017 levels. The government's own new target date for a smaller public service is 2029.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [thespinoff.co.nz](https://thespinoff.co.nz/politics/24-11-2023/the-coalition-deal-at-a-glance) - National-ACT Coalition Agreement, 24 November 2023

### Freeze the adult minimum wage for three years.

**ACT** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Superseded**  
`economy-act-minimum-wage-freeze`

> Three-year minimum wage freeze -> minimum wage rose from $22.70 to $23.95 between April 2023 and April 2026

ACT's policy was not written into the coalition agreements, and the minimum wage was increased in each of the three years.

- Promise (manifesto): [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/programmes/news-extras/story/2018902030/election-2023-rnz-s-guide-to-party-policy) - ACT 2023 election policy

**ACT** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Not delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`economy-act-top-ten-oecd-growth`

> Top 10 OECD for GDP growth -> 1.2% annual growth in the year to June 2026 and unemployment at 5.6%, a decade high

Growth of 1.2 percent in the year to June 2026, following a recession, is not consistent with a top-10 OECD growth ranking, and unemployment rose over the term.

- Promise (manifesto): [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/programmes/news-extras/story/2018902030/election-2023-rnz-s-guide-to-party-policy) - ACT 2023 election policy / alternative budget

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**

A fund of the promised size was established in the first Budget of the term with the promised minister and scope.

## Health

_Between 2017 and 2026 New Zealand rebuilt its health system twice. Labour merged 20 district health boards into a single national agency and created a Māori Health Authority, and the National-led coalition then abolished that authority, restored numerical health targets and reset hospital, medicines and workforce commitments, while wait times, hospital rebuilds and medicines funding remained the most visible tests of both._

### Ensure everyone has access to timely and high quality mental health services, including free counselling for those under 25 years.

**Greens** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`health-greens-free-counselling-under25`

> Free counselling for all under-25s promised -> regional pilot (Wellington region) plus general expansion of primary mental health services

Free counselling for under-25s was delivered only as a regional pilot. The universal entitlement described in the agreement did not eventuate by the end of the term.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [thestandard.org.nz](https://thestandard.org.nz/labour-green-confidence-and-supply-agreement-released/) - Labour-Green Confidence and Supply Agreement 2017
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/free-youth-mental-health-pilot-expands-coverage)
- Evidence: [mhwc.govt.nz](https://www.mhwc.govt.nz/news-and-resources/young-people-missing-out-on-access-to-mental-health-services/)

### Cooperate on improving child and youth wellbeing, including child and youth mental health support.

**Greens** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`health-greens-child-youth-mental-health-2020`

Services expanded. But the agreement set no specific measurable commitment, and independent monitoring found young people's access remained a gap at the end of the term.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [scoop.co.nz](https://www.scoop.co.nz/stories/PA2010/S00117/cooperation-agreement-between-the-nz-labour-party-and-the-green-party-of-aotearoa-nz.htm) - Labour-Green Cooperation Agreement 2020
- Evidence: [mhwc.govt.nz](https://www.mhwc.govt.nz/news-and-resources/young-people-missing-out-on-access-to-mental-health-services/)
- Evidence: [mhwc.govt.nz](https://www.mhwc.govt.nz/assets/Reports/Access-and-Choice-programme/2025/Access-and-Choice-Summary-April-2025.pdf)

### Act on the Health and Disability System Review to replace the district health board structure with a single national health service.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`health-labour-dhb-abolition`

> 20 DHBs -> 1 national agency (Health NZ) from 1 July 2022

The structural change promised was legislated and put in place in full within the term, though the decision to abolish DHBs was announced mid-term in April 2021.

- Promise (manifesto): [ao.parliament.nz](https://ao.parliament.nz/data/health-reforms/about-health-reform) - Labour 2020 commitment to act on the Health and Disability System Review (Simpson Review), commissioned by the Labour government in May 2018
- Evidence: [ao.parliament.nz](https://ao.parliament.nz/data/health-reforms/about-health-reform)
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pae_Ora_(Healthy_Futures)_Act_2022)
- Evidence: [tewhatuora.govt.nz](https://www.tewhatuora.govt.nz/assets/Publications/Annual-Reports/Annual-Report-2024-25-Health-New-Zealand-Te-Whatu-Ora.pdf)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/commissioner-replaces-health-nz-board)

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered**  
`health-labour-maori-health-authority`

> Te Aka Whai Ora established 1 July 2022; $188m allocated in Budget 2022

The authority was established by legislation and operating before the end of the term, as promised.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`health-labour-dunedin-hospital`

> Construction start before 2020 and completion before 2027 promised -> main build not started by 2020, completion pushed to 2029

There was real progress: the site was secured, the business case approved, and enabling works and the outpatient building were underway. But neither the promised construction start date nor the completion date was met.

- Promise (manifesto): [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dunedin_Hospital) - Labour 2017 election campaign commitment on the new Dunedin Hospital

`health-labour-smokefree-generation`

The law promised was passed within the term. Its later repeal was the act of a different government and is recorded separately.

### Extend free breast cancer screening to those aged up to 74.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **In progress**  
`health-nzfirst-breast-screening-74-2023`

> Screening to age 74 promised -> phased rollout started 1 Oct 2025, full coverage due end of 2029

The extension has begun and is funded, but full coverage to age 74 is not due until the end of 2029. This is the same commitment NZ First made in 2017 and did not deliver in the previous era.

- Promise (manifesto): [policy.nz](https://policy.nz/2023/party-vote/policies/health) - NZ First 2023 health policy; also in the National-ACT coalition agreement and the coalition 100-day plan
- Evidence: [tewhatuora.govt.nz](https://www.tewhatuora.govt.nz/news-and-updates/extending-the-age-for-free-breast-screening-to-save-lives)

### Fund birthing units and maternity care properly, including providing for a three day stay for new mothers.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Largely delivered**  
`health-nzfirst-three-day-postnatal-stay`

> 3-day postnatal stay promised -> $34.4m over 4 years in Budget 2026, bill still before Parliament in Aug 2026

Funding is committed and the legislation adopted, but the entitlement was not yet law at the end of the period assessed.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/nzfirst/pages/4462/attachments/original/1700784896/National___NZF_Coalition_Agreement_signed_-_24_Nov_2023.pdf) - National-NZ First Coalition Agreement 2023
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/increased-support-mothers-and-babies)
- Evidence: [midwife.org.nz](https://www.midwife.org.nz/news/34m-in-funding-for-3-day-postnatal-stay-must-be-used-flexibly/)

### End all Covid-19 vaccine mandates still in operation.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Largely delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`gapfill-national-nzf-covid-vaccine-mandates`

> All remaining mandates to end -> government mandates already revoked in 2022; no action on employer mandates by Aug 2026

The promised end state, no government Covid-19 vaccine mandates in operation, holds. But it was already true when the promise was made, and no action was taken on the employer-imposed requirements that remained.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/nzfirst/pages/4462/attachments/original/1700784896/National___NZF_Coalition_Agreement_signed_-_24_Nov_2023.pdf) - National. NZ First Coalition Agreement, 24 November 2023 (Strengthening Democracy and Freedoms)
- Evidence: [hcamag.com](https://www.hcamag.com/nz/specialisation/employment-law/will-new-zealand-end-covid-19-vaccine-mandates-in-workplaces/489945)
- Evidence: [stuff.co.nz](https://www.stuff.co.nz/politics/350459889/confusion-surrounds-coalition-s-covid-19-vaccine-mandate-pledge-nz-first-still-working-through-the-details)

### Require Medsafe to approve new pharmaceuticals within 30 days of them being approved by at least two overseas regulatory agencies.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`gapfill-national-nzf-medsafe-30-day-approval`

> 30-day approval after 2 overseas regulators -> legislated 12 November 2025

The 30-day rule and the two-overseas-regulator test were legislated in the terms set out in the agreement.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/nzfirst/pages/4462/attachments/original/1700784896/National___NZF_Coalition_Agreement_signed_-_24_Nov_2023.pdf) - National. NZ First Coalition Agreement, 24 November 2023 (Health)
- Evidence: [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/news/political/578629/law-ensuring-approval-of-new-medicines-allowed-in-other-countries-passes-in-parliament)
- Evidence: [health.govt.nz](https://www.health.govt.nz/regulation-legislation/medicines-legislation/regulating-medicines-medical-devices-and-natural-health-products/documents-on-the-medical-products-bill)

### Repeal the Therapeutic Products Act 2023.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`gapfill-national-nzf-repeal-therapeutic-products-act`

> Repeal promised -> Therapeutic Products Act 2023 repealed 13 December 2024

The Act was repealed as promised. The promise was to repeal, not to replace.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/nzfirst/pages/4462/attachments/original/1700784896/National___NZF_Coalition_Agreement_signed_-_24_Nov_2023.pdf) - National. NZ First Coalition Agreement, 24 November 2023 (Health)
- Evidence: [legislation.govt.nz](https://www.legislation.govt.nz/act/public/2024/0055/latest/whole.html)
- Evidence: [scoop.co.nz](https://www.scoop.co.nz/stories/PA2412/S00157/therapeutic-products-act-repeal-bill-passed.htm)
- Evidence: [bellgully.com](https://www.bellgully.com/insights/therapeutic-products-business-as-usual-for-the-moment-but-the-rationale-for-reform-remains/)

### Fund 13 new cancer treatments, at $280 million over four years ($70m a year), starting 1 July 2024, paid for by reinstating the $5 prescription co-payment.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Largely delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`health-national-cancer-drugs-13`

> 13 named treatments and $280m over 4 years promised -> $604m over 4 years announced June 2024, up to 7 of the 13 funded plus substitutes

More money and more medicines were funded in the end than promised. But it came a year late, not on the promised start date, and only about half the specific named treatments were funded.

- Promise (manifesto): [thespinoff.co.nz](https://thespinoff.co.nz/politics/05-06-2024/the-full-timeline-of-nationals-broken-cancer-drugs-promise) - National 2023 campaign announcement, 21 August 2023

- Evidence: [pharmac.govt.nz](https://www.pharmac.govt.nz/news-and-resources/news/update-on-new-medicines-funding-after-the-budget-uplift)

### Increase Pharmac funding by $724 million over four years.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`health-national-pharmac-funding`

> $724m over 4 years promised -> $604m for new medicines alone (June 2024) on top of Budget 2024 baseline increases

Pharmac's funding rose by more than the promised amount across the term, counting the Budget 2024 baseline uplift and the June 2024 $604m new-medicines pre-commitment.

- Promise (manifesto): [policy.nz](https://policy.nz/2023/party-vote/policies/health) - National 2023 health policy
- Evidence: [pharmac.govt.nz](https://www.pharmac.govt.nz/news-and-resources/publications/corporate-publications/year-in-review/year-in-review/budget-increase-for-more-medicines)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/transformative-investment-cancer-treatments-and-more-new-medicines)

### Repeal the Smokefree Environments and Regulated Products (Smoked Tobacco) Amendment Act 2022 to remove the requirements for denicotinisation and the reduction in retail outlets.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`health-national-smokefree-repeal`

> 3 smokefree measures repealed, in force 6 March 2024

The repeal was passed within the 100-day plan window, and in the terms set out in the coalition agreements.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/actnz/mailings/6945/attachments/original/National_ACT_Agreement.pdf) - National-ACT Coalition Agreement 2023 (also in the National-NZ First agreement and the coalition 100-day plan)

### Set five major health targets for the health system, including for wait times and cancer treatment, and publish performance against them.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`health-national-five-health-targets`

> 5 health targets promised -> 5 set March 2024 and reported quarterly since

The targets were set and are reported publicly each quarter, which is what the commitment required. Whether the targets themselves are met is tracked separately.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [national.org.nz](https://www.national.org.nz/policies/targeting-better-health-outcomes) - Coalition 100-day plan and National-NZ First Coalition Agreement 2023

- Evidence: [dpmc.govt.nz](https://www.dpmc.govt.nz/our-programmes/government-targets)

### 95% of patients to be admitted, discharged or transferred from an emergency department within six hours.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`health-national-ed-six-hour-target`

> 95% within 6 hours promised -> 68.0% (Sep 2023 baseline) to 74.4% (Mar 2026 quarter)

Term unfinished. There is measurable improvement on the baseline, but the target remains a long way off as at March 2026.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [dpmc.govt.nz](https://www.dpmc.govt.nz/our-programmes/government-targets) - Government Targets (Target 1), set March 2024

- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/progress-continues-across-all-five-health-targets)

### 95% of people to wait less than four months for elective treatment.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`health-national-elective-wait-target`

> 95% within 4 months promised -> 62.0% (Sep 2023 baseline) to 64.9% (Mar 2026 quarter)

Term unfinished. Volumes and the share treated within four months have risen from the baseline, but the target is far from being met.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [dpmc.govt.nz](https://www.dpmc.govt.nz/our-programmes/government-targets) - Government Targets (Target 2), set March 2024

### Build the new Dunedin Hospital, including $30 million to restore beds, operating theatres and a PET scanner that had been cut from the design.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Partly delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`health-national-dunedin-hospital`

> 351 beds in the new build vs 396 in the old hospital; cost about $1.88b, opening 2031

The hospital is being built and construction is well advanced. But the scope was reduced rather than restored, and the promised restoration of beds and theatres did not occur.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`health-national-waikato-medical-school`

> $82.85m Crown funding; 120 extra doctor training places a year from 2028

The commitment was to progress the school subject to a cost-benefit analysis. That analysis was done, and the school was approved and funded within the term.

Financial incentives for graduate nurses were introduced and graduate placements grew. But this was a targeted recruitment payment rather than the general study-and-stay bonding scheme described in the 100-day plan.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [russellmcveagh.com](https://www.russellmcveagh.com/media/h1whr1gg/national-act-nz-first-coalition-government-policies-public-services.pdf) - Coalition 100-day plan 2023

## Education

_Across the two eras New Zealand's schools and tertiary system were pulled in opposite directions. Labour scrapped National Standards and charter schools, made a year of tertiary study free and merged the polytechnics, while the National-led coalition reinstated charter schools, unwound the polytechnic merger, rewrote the curriculum around structured literacy and maths, and eventually abolished Fees Free altogether._

### Make tertiary education more affordable for students and reduce the number of students living in financial hardship.

**Greens** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`education-greens-tertiary-affordability`

> 1 year fee-free delivered from 2018; planned extension to 3 years abandoned in 2020

A significant affordability measure was delivered and sustained, but the expansion stopped in 2020. Independent evaluation found little effect on students facing the greatest hardship.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [thestandard.org.nz](https://thestandard.org.nz/labour-green-confidence-and-supply-agreement-released/) - Labour-Green Confidence and Supply Agreement 2017
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fees_Free)
- Evidence: [theconversation.com](https://theconversation.com/nzs-costly-fees-free-scheme-did-little-to-widen-access-to-tertiary-education-new-study-282587)

### Make the first year of tertiary study or training fee-free from 2018 (up to about $12,000), then extend free study to two years and then to three years.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`education-labour-fees-free-three-years`

> 3 years free study promised -> 1 year delivered; extension abandoned in 2020

One year of free study was delivered and kept for the whole term. The promised extension to three years was dropped and never happened.

- Promise (manifesto): [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fees_Free) - Labour 2017 election campaign policy on tertiary fees
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fees_Free)
- Evidence: [thespinoff.co.nz](https://thespinoff.co.nz/the-bulletin/11-05-2026/the-troubled-life-and-death-of-the-fees-free-tertiary-scheme)
- Evidence: [theconversation.com](https://theconversation.com/nzs-costly-fees-free-scheme-did-little-to-widen-access-to-tertiary-education-new-study-282587)

### Scrap National Standards for literacy and numeracy, and primary school league tables.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered**  
`education-labour-national-standards`

Done in full, immediately and as promised.

- Promise (manifesto): [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sixth_Labour_Government_of_New_Zealand) - Labour 2017 election policy on assessment in schools
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sixth_Labour_Government_of_New_Zealand)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/teaching-basics-brilliantly-new-curriculum-resources-and-assessment-tools)

### End the charter (partnership) school model, stop any new charter schools and bring existing ones into the state system.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered**  
`education-labour-close-charter-schools`

> 11 partnership schools with 1,441 students -> all converted to state schools by 2019

The charter school model was closed within the term, exactly as promised.

- Promise (manifesto): [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sixth_Labour_Government_of_New_Zealand) - Labour 2017 election policy on partnership (charter) schools
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sixth_Labour_Government_of_New_Zealand)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/over-200-roll-growth-first-seven-charter-schools)

### Introduce and scale up a free healthy school lunch programme for students in the schools with the greatest need.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered**  
`education-labour-school-lunches`

> 2019 pilot -> more than 200,000 students fed daily, at about $8 per student

The programme was set up and scaled to more than 200,000 students a day within the term.

- Promise (manifesto): [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sixth_Labour_Government_of_New_Zealand) - Labour's Ka Ora, Ka Ako Healthy School Lunches programme, piloted 2019 and expanded at Budget 2020
- Evidence: [ao.parliament.nz](https://ao.parliament.nz/2026/school-lunches/overview.htm)
- Evidence: [thespinoff.co.nz](https://thespinoff.co.nz/politics/30-06-2026/school-lunch-inquiry-finds-long-list-of-failures)

### Merge the 16 polytechnics and institutes of technology into a single national institute of skills and technology by April 2020.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered**  
`education-labour-te-pukenga-merger`

> 16 polytechnics merged into 1 entity from 1 April 2020 -> 10 re-established as regional institutions from 1 January 2026

The merger promised was legislated and the single entity created on schedule. Its later unwinding was the act of a different government.

- Promise (manifesto): [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sixth_Labour_Government_of_New_Zealand) - Labour's Reform of Vocational Education, announced 2019 and carried into the 2020 term
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sixth_Labour_Government_of_New_Zealand)
- Evidence: [education.govt.nz](https://www.education.govt.nz/news/regional-polytechnics-be-re-established-2026)

### Reintroduce partnership schools and introduce a policy to allow state schools to become partnership schools. Budget 2024 funded $153 million over four years for 15 new charter schools and 35 state school conversions.

**ACT** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`education-act-charter-schools`

> 15 new schools and 35 conversions funded -> 16 charter schools and 1,471 students by March 2026; only 6 state schools interested in conversion in the April 2025 round

The model was reinstated in law and more charter schools are now open than under the previous partnership scheme. But the funded target of 35 state school conversions was nowhere near met.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/actnz/mailings/6945/attachments/original/National_ACT_Agreement.pdf) - National-ACT Coalition Agreement 2023 and coalition 100-day plan
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/over-200-roll-growth-first-seven-charter-schools)
- Evidence: [nzeiteriuroa.org.nz](https://www.nzeiteriuroa.org.nz/assets/downloads/Charter-schools/Charter-schools-fact-sheet-October-2024.pdf)
- Evidence: [1news.co.nz](https://www.1news.co.nz/2025/04/27/just-six-state-schools-interested-in-charter-conversion-in-latest-round/)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/three-new-charter-schools-will-give-students-new-options-term-1-2026)

### Improve the cost-effectiveness of the school lunch programme.

**ACT** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Partly delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`education-act-school-lunch-cost`

> About $8 per meal -> $3 per meal target; $134m savings claimed for 2025, but only about 50% of meals met nutrition standards May-Dec 2025

Large cost savings were achieved and the programme kept feeding more than 200,000 students a day. But the Auditor-General could not verify the savings claims, and found serious nutrition, waste and delivery failures.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/actnz/mailings/6945/attachments/original/National_ACT_Agreement.pdf) - National-ACT Coalition Agreement 2023
- Evidence: [ao.parliament.nz](https://ao.parliament.nz/2026/school-lunches/overview.htm)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/smarter-lunch-programme-feeds-more-costs-less)
- Evidence: [thespinoff.co.nz](https://thespinoff.co.nz/politics/30-06-2026/school-lunch-inquiry-finds-long-list-of-failures)

### Amend the Education and Training Act 2020 so that tertiary education providers receiving taxpayer funding must commit to a free speech policy.

**ACT** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Largely delivered**  
`gapfill-national-act-tertiary-free-speech-policy`

> Free speech policy required of taxpayer-funded tertiary providers -> legislated Nov 2025, university statements adopted by April 2026

The Act was passed and policies are in place. But the duty applies to universities rather than to all tertiary providers receiving taxpayer funding, as the agreement was worded.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/actnz/mailings/6945/attachments/original/National_ACT_Agreement.pdf) - National. ACT Coalition Agreement, 24 November 2023
- Evidence: [education.govt.nz](https://www.education.govt.nz/our-work/legislation/recent-acts-passed/academic-freedom-and-freedom-expression-provisions-universities)
- Evidence: [newsroom.co.nz](https://newsroom.co.nz/2026/04/10/free-at-last-universities-adopt-govt-mandated-freedom-of-expression-statements/)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/strengthening-free-speech-universities)

### Stop first year Fees Free and replace it with a final year Fees Free, with no change before 2025.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Largely delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`education-nzfirst-fees-free-final-year`

> First year Fees Free -> final year Fees Free from Jan 2025 -> scheme abolished from end of 2026

The promised switch to final-year Fees Free was delivered on time in 2025. The coalition then abolished the scheme entirely in 2026, so the promised entitlement no longer exists at the end of the term.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/nzfirst/pages/4462/attachments/original/1700784896/National___NZF_Coalition_Agreement_signed_-_24_Nov_2023.pdf) - National-NZ First Coalition Agreement 2023 (also in the National-ACT agreement and the 100-day plan)
- Evidence: [ird.govt.nz](https://www.ird.govt.nz/updates/news-folder/2026/fees-free-will-finish-at-the-end-of-2026)
- Evidence: [thespinoff.co.nz](https://thespinoff.co.nz/the-bulletin/11-05-2026/the-troubled-life-and-death-of-the-fees-free-tertiary-scheme)
- Evidence: [newsroom.co.nz](https://newsroom.co.nz/2026/05/28/scrapping-fees-free-saves-govt-over-1b-in-budget/)
- Evidence: [1news.co.nz](https://www.1news.co.nz/2026/05/11/fees-free-scheme-failed-to-get-more-students-into-tertiary-education-govt/)

### Restore balance to the Aotearoa New Zealand's Histories curriculum. Refocus the curriculum on academic achievement rather than ideology, including removing and replacing the gender, sexuality and relationship-based education guidelines.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **In progress**  
`education-nzfirst-histories-curriculum`

> Rewritten social sciences curriculum released Aug 2026, in force from 2027-2028

Term unfinished. The rewritten social sciences and science curricula were released in August 2026 but do not take effect until 2027 and 2028.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/nzfirst/pages/4462/attachments/original/1700784896/National___NZF_Coalition_Agreement_signed_-_24_Nov_2023.pdf) - National-NZ First Coalition Agreement 2023 (also in the National-ACT agreement)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/updated-science-and-social-sciences-curriculum-prepares-students-changing-world)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/teaching-basics-brilliantly-new-curriculum-resources-and-assessment-tools)

### Ban the use of cellphones in schools.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`education-national-phone-ban`

> Phone ban in force from Term 2 2024

The ban became a legal requirement for all state schools within the promised 100 days, and took effect in Term 2 2024.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/backing-our-kids-learn-basics) - Coalition 100-day plan, National-ACT Coalition Agreement and National-NZ First Coalition Agreement 2023
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/backing-our-kids-learn-basics)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/first-steps-100-day-plan-education-removing-distractions-and-teaching-basics-brilliantly)

### Require primary and intermediate schools to teach an hour of reading, an hour of writing and an hour of maths a day, starting in 2024.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`education-national-hour-a-day-basics`

> 1 hour each of reading, writing and maths a day from 2024; 98% of schools teaching the new English and maths learning areas by Term 1 2025

The requirement was imposed on the promised timetable, and independent review found near-universal uptake of the associated teaching changes.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/backing-our-kids-learn-basics) - Coalition 100-day plan, National-ACT Coalition Agreement and National-NZ First Coalition Agreement 2023
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/backing-our-kids-learn-basics)
- Evidence: [evidence.ero.govt.nz](https://www.evidence.ero.govt.nz/media/m5maujol/a-new-chapter-how-well-are-the-changes-to-english-and-maths-going-research-report.pdf)

### Appoint an expert group to redesign the English and maths curricula for primary school students. Refocus the curriculum on academic achievement, including a structured approach to teaching reading.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`education-national-curriculum-rewrite`

> New English and maths curricula compulsory from Term 1 2026; other learning areas phased in 2027-2028

The curriculum rewrite was completed and made compulsory for English and maths within the term. The remaining subjects were released and scheduled.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [russellmcveagh.com](https://www.russellmcveagh.com/media/h1whr1gg/national-act-nz-first-coalition-government-policies-public-services.pdf) - Coalition 100-day plan and National-ACT Coalition Agreement 2023
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/teaching-basics-brilliantly-new-curriculum-resources-and-assessment-tools)
- Evidence: [evidence.ero.govt.nz](https://www.evidence.ero.govt.nz/media/m5maujol/a-new-chapter-how-well-are-the-changes-to-english-and-maths-going-research-report.pdf)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/updated-science-and-social-sciences-curriculum-prepares-students-changing-world)

### Introduce standardised assessments in schools and require regular reporting to parents.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`education-national-standardised-assessment`

> Twice-yearly standardised assessment for Years 3-8 and a mandatory phonics check from 2026

Mandatory standardised assessment and reporting were in place from 2026, as the agreement required. The wider qualifications replacement is scheduled beyond this term.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/actnz/mailings/6945/attachments/original/National_ACT_Agreement.pdf) - National-ACT Coalition Agreement 2023
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/teaching-basics-brilliantly-new-curriculum-resources-and-assessment-tools)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/government-confirms-next-steps-new-senior-secondary-qualification)

### Begin disestablishing Te Pūkenga and return polytechnics to regional governance.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Largely delivered**  
`education-national-disestablish-te-pukenga`

> 16 merged polytechnics -> 10 re-established regionally from 1 Jan 2026, 4 still in a transitional federation

The merger has been unwound and ten polytechnics are independent again. But four institutions remained under the transitional entity at the start of 2026, so the change was not complete.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [russellmcveagh.com](https://www.russellmcveagh.com/media/h1whr1gg/national-act-nz-first-coalition-government-policies-public-services.pdf) - Coalition 100-day plan and National-ACT Coalition Agreement 2023
- Evidence: [education.govt.nz](https://www.education.govt.nz/news/regional-polytechnics-be-re-established-2026)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/delivering-our-promise-disestablish-te-p%C5%ABkenga)
- Evidence: [thespinoff.co.nz](https://thespinoff.co.nz/politics/17-03-2025/more-than-150-jobs-cut-and-one-campus-closed-as-te-pukenga-disestablishment-looms)

### 80% of students to be present for more than 90% of the term. Enforce compulsory education by collecting and publishing attendance data centrally.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **In progress**  
`education-national-attendance-target`

> 80% attending regularly promised -> 59.0% (Term 1 2023) to 68.8% (Term 1 2026)

Term unfinished. The reporting and enforcement machinery was delivered and attendance has risen almost 10 points from the baseline, but the 80% target has not been reached.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [dpmc.govt.nz](https://www.dpmc.govt.nz/our-programmes/government-targets) - Government Targets (Target 6), set March 2024; truancy commitments in the National-ACT and National-NZ First coalition agreements
- Evidence: [dpmc.govt.nz](https://www.dpmc.govt.nz/sites/default/files/2026-06/gt-factsheet-target-6-mar26.pdf)

- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/attendance-rates-increase-again-0)

### 80% of Year 8 students to be at or above the expected curriculum level for their age in reading, writing and maths by December 2030.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **In progress**  
`education-national-year8-achievement-target`

> 80% of Year 8 students at or above curriculum level by December 2030

Term unfinished and the target date is 2030. The delivery machinery has been built, but the outcome cannot yet be judged.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [dpmc.govt.nz](https://www.dpmc.govt.nz/our-programmes/government-targets) - Coalition 100-day plan and Government Targets (Target 7), set March 2024
- Evidence: [dpmc.govt.nz](https://www.dpmc.govt.nz/our-programmes/government-targets)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/teaching-basics-brilliantly-new-curriculum-resources-and-assessment-tools)

## Law & Order

_Two governments took opposite approaches to crime and punishment: the Sixth Labour Government set out to shrink the prison population and reform sentencing, while the Sixth National Government campaigned on reversing that direction with tougher sentencing, gang laws and more police, and both promised large increases in police numbers that proved hard to deliver on time._

### Reform sentencing, bail and parole laws to enable the gradual replacement of most prisons with community-based rehabilitation.

**Greens** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Not delivered**  
`law-order-labour-greens-replace-prisons`

The Greens' central sentencing, bail and parole reform was not enacted. The policy direction was reversed after 2023.

- Promise (manifesto): [greens.org.nz](https://www.greens.org.nz/justice_2020) - Green Party 2020 justice policy
- Evidence: [nzherald.co.nz](https://www.nzherald.co.nz/nz/election-2023-labour-dumps-prison-population-reduction-target/AAL2EXLUGBFNHJ27VJSRS2EPHY/)
- Evidence: [corrections.govt.nz](https://www.corrections.govt.nz/resources/statistics/quarterly_prison_statistics/prison_facts_and_statistics_-_march_2026)

### Lead a whole-of-government response to family violence and sexual violence, including coordinating budget proposals across agencies, under a new dedicated ministerial portfolio.

**Greens** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered**  
`gapfill-greens-te-aorerekura-fvsv`

> First national FVSV strategy -> Te Aorerekura launched December 2021 with $114.5m over four years in Budget 2022

The portfolio was created, the national strategy was published within the term and dedicated multi-year funding was secured for it.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [scoop.co.nz](https://www.scoop.co.nz/stories/PA2010/S00117/cooperation-agreement-between-the-nz-labour-party-and-the-green-party-of-aotearoa-nz.htm) - Labour-Greens Cooperation Agreement 2020. Marama Davidson appointed Minister for the Prevention of Family and Sexual Violence, leading the whole-of-government response and coordinating related budget proposals
- Evidence: [justice.govt.nz](https://www.justice.govt.nz/about/news-and-media/news/te-aorerekura-the-national-strategy-to-eliminate-family-violence-and-sexual-violence-launched/)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/budget-2022-investing-eliminate-violence-our-homes-and-communities)

### Reduce New Zealand's prison population by 30 percent over 15 years.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Partly delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`law-order-labour-prison-population-30-percent`

> 30 pct cut over 15 years promised -> muster 10,645 (Mar 2018) to 8,893 (Sep 2023), about 16 pct

The muster fell about 16 percent, well short of 30 percent on the headline measure. The target was also dropped before the 15-year horizon.

- Promise (manifesto): [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/news/on-the-inside/364632/reducing-prison-numbers-one-mighty-challenge-for-little) - Labour government criminal justice target announced by Justice Minister Andrew Little, 2018 (Hapaitia te Oranga Tangata programme)
- Evidence: [corrections.govt.nz](https://www.corrections.govt.nz/resources/statistics/quarterly_prison_statistics/prison_stats_march_2018)
- Evidence: [corrections.govt.nz](https://www.corrections.govt.nz/resources/statistics/quarterly_prison_statistics/prison_stats_september_2023)
- Evidence: [nzherald.co.nz](https://www.nzherald.co.nz/nz/election-2023-labour-dumps-prison-population-reduction-target/AAL2EXLUGBFNHJ27VJSRS2EPHY/)

### Repeal the three strikes sentencing regime.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Largely delivered**  
`law-order-labour-repeal-three-strikes`

> repeal promised 2017 -> blocked June 2018, enacted 15 Aug 2022

The repeal happened as promised, but only in the second term. The coalition partner blocked it for the whole of the first term.

- Promise (manifesto): [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/news/political/359325/labour-s-three-strikes-repeal-off-the-table) - Labour 2017 justice policy; repeal announced by Justice Minister Andrew Little in 2018
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_Strikes_Legislation_Repeal_Act_2022)
- Evidence: [justice.govt.nz](https://www.justice.govt.nz/justice-sector-policy/key-initiatives/three-strikes-law/)

### Undertake a comprehensive reform of the criminal justice system, guided by an independent advisory group and a public criminal justice summit.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`law-order-labour-criminal-justice-reform-programme`

The review process promised was set up and reported. Its central recommendations were not turned into legislation within the term.

- Promise (manifesto): [justice.govt.nz](https://www.justice.govt.nz/about/news-and-media/news/safe-and-effective-justice-launched/) - Labour 2017 justice policy, delivered as Hapaitia te Oranga Tangata: Safe and Effective Justice (August 2018)
- Evidence: [nzherald.co.nz](https://www.nzherald.co.nz/nz/election-2023-labour-dumps-prison-population-reduction-target/AAL2EXLUGBFNHJ27VJSRS2EPHY/)
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_Strikes_Legislation_Repeal_Act_2022)

### Ban military-style semi-automatic firearms and assault rifles, buy them back from owners, and establish a national firearms registry.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`law-order-labour-firearms-ban-buyback-registry`

> ban and buyback promised -> about 51,000 firearms bought back for about NZ$100m; registry live 24 June 2023

The ban, the buyback and the registry were all legislated and implemented within the term, on the timetable set out.

- Promise (manifesto): [abc.net.au](https://www.abc.net.au/news/2019-12-22/new-zealand-gun-buyback-ends-with-50000-firearms-handed-in/11821402) - Government commitment announced by Prime Minister Jacinda Ardern, March 2019, after the Christchurch attacks; carried through in the Arms Legislation Act 2020
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arms_Legislation_Act_2020)

- Evidence: [firearmssafetyauthority.govt.nz](https://www.firearmssafetyauthority.govt.nz/news-and-publications/news-and-announcements/10000th-person-signs-firearms-registry)

### Create a specific ram-raid offence. Allow 12 and 13 year olds to be dealt with in the Youth Court for ram raiding, and target adults who recruit children into ram raids.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Not delivered**  
`law-order-labour-ram-raid-offence`

> new ram-raid offence promised -> bill lapsed after first reading (29 Aug 2023), discontinued Feb 2025

The bill was introduced and passed only its first reading. The promised offence never became law.

- Promise (manifesto): [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ram_Raid_Offending_and_Related_Measures_Amendment_Bill) - Labour government policy announcement, 2023 (Ram Raid Offending and Related Measures Amendment Bill)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/multi-million-dollar-package-tackle-retail-crime-and-reoffending)

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Abandoned**  
`gapfill-national-labour-hate-speech-protections`

> Protections promised on 4 additional grounds -> none legislated by October 2023

The commitment was first narrowed to one ground and then explicitly removed from the legislative programme by the promising party in February 2023.
