### Commit to re-entry of the Pike River mine.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Largely delivered**  
`gapfill-national-labour-pike-river-reentry`

> Re-entry promised -> 2.26km of drift recovered by 17 Feb 2021; main workings not entered; about $48m spent

Re-entry happened and the full drift was recovered and examined. But the main mine workings where the men died were never entered, and no bodies were recovered.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [mcguinnessinstitute.org](https://www.mcguinnessinstitute.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/WP202007-202009-AnalysisOfThe2017Labour-FINAL.pdf) - Labour. NZ First Coalition Agreement 2017 (priority 54: commit to re-entry to Pike River)

- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pike_River_Recovery_Agency)

### Respond to the Royal Commission of Inquiry into the terrorist attack on the Christchurch masjidain with a view to eradicating violent extremism.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Largely delivered**  
`gapfill-national-labour-christchurch-royal-commission-response`

> 44 recommendations accepted -> 36 implemented or integrated, 8 not progressed (confirmed 2 August 2024)

A full response was mounted and most recommendations were implemented. Eight were still outstanding at the end of the term and were later dropped.

- Promise (manifesto): [s3.ap-southeast-2.amazonaws.com](https://s3.ap-southeast-2.amazonaws.com/nzdoctor.files/production/public/2020-10/Labour_Manifesto_2020.pdf) - Labour 2020 manifesto

### Strive towards adding 1800 new Police officers over three years, with a serious focus on combatting organised crime and drugs.

**NZ First** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Largely delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`law-order-labour-nzf-1800-police`

> 1,800 new police over 3 years -> 1,800th new constable sworn in 21 Nov 2019 (gross); net 1,800 above attrition not confirmed in that window

The gross recruitment count promised in the agreement was met inside three years. The net increase in officers after attrition took much longer and was disputed throughout.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [police.govt.nz](https://www.police.govt.nz/about-us/publication/striving-towards-1800-new-police) - Labour-NZ First coalition agreement, October 2017

### Establish a Criminal Cases Review Commission to investigate possible miscarriages of justice.

**NZ First** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered**  
`law-order-labour-nzf-criminal-cases-review-commission`

The Commission was legislated for and stood up within the coalition's term.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [mcguinnessinstitute.org](https://www.mcguinnessinstitute.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/WP202007-202009-AnalysisOfThe2017Labour-FINAL.pdf) - Labour-NZ First coalition agreement, October 2017

### Restore the Three Strikes law, with amendments including a limited benefit for guilty pleas.

**ACT** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`law-order-national-act-restore-three-strikes`

> three strikes restored -> Act in force 17 June 2025, covering 42 offences with 12 and 24 month sentence thresholds

The law was restored with the amendments the agreement itself specified, and is in force.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [russellmcveagh.com](https://www.russellmcveagh.com/media/vb5pqsl4/national-act-nz-first-coalition-government-policies-law-and-order.pdf) - National-ACT coalition agreement, 24 November 2023

### Abolish the previous government's prisoner reduction target, and increase funding for Corrections to ensure there is enough prison capacity.

**ACT** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Largely delivered**  
`law-order-national-act-prison-capacity`

> prison capacity funding promised -> about NZ$1.9b in Budget 2024; 596 beds opened June 2025; muster 8,893 (Sep 2023) to 11,255 (Mar 2026)

The target was abolished, and major capacity funding and one large new facility were delivered. But most of the promised extra beds are not due until 2029, and the muster has grown faster than capacity.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [russellmcveagh.com](https://www.russellmcveagh.com/media/vb5pqsl4/national-act-nz-first-coalition-government-policies-law-and-order.pdf) - National-ACT coalition agreement and the 100-day plan, November 2023

**ACT** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`law-order-national-act-arms-act-rewrite`

Both the Part 6 replacement and the full Arms Act rewrite were passed inside the term.

### Commit to training no fewer than 500 new frontline police within the first two years.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Not delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`law-order-national-nzf-500-police`

> 500 new frontline police within 2 years -> deadline of 27 Nov 2025 missed; projected date pushed to Sep 2026

The commitment carried an explicit two-year deadline, which passed on 27 November 2025 without being met. The target had still not been confirmed as reached by August 2026.

### Move responsibility for the Arms Act to the Ministry of Justice and review the effectiveness of the Firearms Registry by June 2024.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Largely delivered**  
`law-order-national-nzf-firearms-registry-review`

> registry review due June 2024 -> published May 2025; registry retained

The transfer and the review both happened, but the review was published about eleven months after the deadline in the agreement.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [russellmcveagh.com](https://www.russellmcveagh.com/media/vb5pqsl4/national-act-nz-first-coalition-government-policies-law-and-order.pdf) - National-NZ First coalition agreement, 24 November 2023

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`gapfill-national-nzf-hate-speech-ruled-out`

> Hate speech legislation ruled out -> no incitement bill introduced by August 2026

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**

> gang patch ban promised -> Gangs Act in force 21 Nov 2024; 337 charges and 76 patches seized by 24 Feb 2025

The legislation promised was passed and brought into force within the first year of the term and is being enforced.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [russellmcveagh.com](https://www.russellmcveagh.com/media/vb5pqsl4/national-act-nz-first-coalition-government-policies-law-and-order.pdf) - National-ACT and National-NZ First coalition agreements and the 100-day plan, November 2023
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gangs_Act_2024)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/first-arrests-made-under-gang-patch-ban)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/more-gangs-added-gangs-act-list)

### Amend sentencing law to limit the discounts judges can apply, and to remove concurrent sentences for offending committed while on bail, parole or in custody.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`law-order-national-sentencing-discount-cap`

> cap on sentencing discounts promised -> 40 pct cap on personal mitigating factors, in force 29 June 2025

The discount cap and the cumulative sentencing changes promised were legislated and are in force.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/nzfirst/pages/4462/attachments/original/1700784896/National___NZF_Coalition_Agreement_signed_-_24_Nov_2023.pdf) - National-ACT and National-NZ First coalition agreements, November 2023
- Evidence: [justice.govt.nz](https://www.justice.govt.nz/about/news-and-media/news/sentencing-act-changes-now-in-force/)
- Evidence: [legislation.govt.nz](https://www.legislation.govt.nz/act/public/2025/0013/13.0/LMS989992.html)

### Amend the Sentencing Act so the needs of victims and communities are prioritised, and stop taxpayer funding for section 27 cultural reports.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`law-order-national-victims-sentencing`

Both the cultural reports change and the victim-focused sentencing amendments were made within the term.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [russellmcveagh.com](https://www.russellmcveagh.com/media/vb5pqsl4/national-act-nz-first-coalition-government-policies-law-and-order.pdf) - National-ACT and National-NZ First coalition agreements, November 2023
- Evidence: [justice.govt.nz](https://www.justice.govt.nz/about/news-and-media/news/sentencing-act-changes-now-in-force/)
- Evidence: [nzherald.co.nz](https://www.nzherald.co.nz/nz/politics/government-scraps-labours-target-of-reducing-the-prison-population-and-funding-for-cultural-reports/3K3NNZD5QFCSZD5YMOW6Y5TNGA/)

### Establish military-style academies for serious repeat youth offenders, backed by legislation creating Young Serious Offender declarations.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Partly delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`law-order-national-boot-camps`

> military-style academies promised -> 10-person pilot Jul 2024 to Aug 2025; 7 of 10 reoffended; second programme from 9 Mar 2026

A pilot ran and a second programme was scheduled. But the enabling legislation had not completed passage, and the promised permanent academy regime was not yet operating at scale.

- Promise (manifesto): [orangatamariki.govt.nz](https://www.orangatamariki.govt.nz/youth-justice/military-style-academies/) - National 2023 youth justice policy, delivered as Oranga Tamariki Military-Style Academies
- Evidence: [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/news/top/578157/implementation-of-boot-camp-rushed-report-concludes)
- Evidence: [1news.co.nz](https://www.1news.co.nz/2025/11/07/military-style-boot-camp-pilot-too-rushed-and-too-small-report/)

### 20,000 fewer people are victims of an assault, robbery or sexual assault.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`law-order-national-violent-crime-target`

> 20,000 fewer violent crime victims promised -> about 46,000 fewer (185,000 in Oct 2023 to 138,795 in Feb 2026)

The reduction reported is more than double the promised 20,000, and has held across successive quarterly reports.

- Promise (manifesto): [dpmc.govt.nz](https://www.dpmc.govt.nz/our-programmes/government-targets) - National 2023 campaign commitment, adopted as Government Target 4
- Evidence: [dpmc.govt.nz](https://www.dpmc.govt.nz/sites/default/files/2026-06/gt-factsheet-target-4-mar26.pdf)
- Evidence: [dpmc.govt.nz](https://www.dpmc.govt.nz/sites/default/files/2026-03/gt-factsheet-target-4-dec25.pdf)

### A 15 percent reduction in the total number of children and young people with serious and persistent offending behaviour.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`law-order-national-youth-offending-target`

> 15 pct cut in serious repeat youth offenders promised -> 25 pct cut (1,100 in Jun 2023 to 829 in Mar 2026)

The reported reduction of 25 percent exceeds the promised 15 percent, ahead of the target date.

- Promise (manifesto): [dpmc.govt.nz](https://www.dpmc.govt.nz/our-programmes/government-targets) - National 2023 campaign commitment, adopted as Government Target 3
- Evidence: [dpmc.govt.nz](https://www.dpmc.govt.nz/sites/default/files/2026-06/gt-factsheet-target-3-mar26.pdf)
- Evidence: [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/news/indonz/542587/ram-raids-decline-almost-60-percent-year-on-year)
- Evidence: [dpmc.govt.nz](https://www.dpmc.govt.nz/sites/default/files/2026-03/gt-factsheet-target-3-dec25.pdf)

## Climate & Emissions

_Between 2017 and 2026 New Zealand legislated a net-zero-by-2050 framework and an independent Climate Change Commission, then saw successive governments argue over how fast and by what means to meet it. Labour framing climate as its generation's "nuclear-free moment" while the National-led coalition removed agriculture from carbon pricing, lowered the methane target and reopened offshore petroleum exploration._

### Deliver a Zero Carbon Act, set up an independent Climate Change Commission and establish a Green Investment Fund. These were the Greens' price for confidence and supply.

**Greens** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered**  
`climate-greens-zero-carbon-commission`

> 3 institutions promised -> Zero Carbon Act, Climate Change Commission and Green Investment Fund all established

All three named institutions were created within the 2017-2020 term.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [greens.org.nz](https://www.greens.org.nz/greens_welcome_labour_s_climate_commitments_will_push_for_more_action_to_match_the_scale_of_the_crisis) - Labour-Greens Confidence and Supply Agreement 2017 (climate commitments)
- Evidence: [legislation.govt.nz](https://www.legislation.govt.nz/act/public/2019/0061/latest/LMS183848.html)
- Evidence: [greens.org.nz](https://www.greens.org.nz/greens_welcome_labour_s_climate_commitments_will_push_for_more_action_to_match_the_scale_of_the_crisis)

### Require a climate change lens (a climate impact assessment) on major government decisions and legislation.

**Greens** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Largely delivered**  
`climate-greens-climate-impact-assessments`

The climate lens was implemented. It came as Cabinet-office guidance on qualifying proposals, not the statutory across-the-board requirement the Greens originally sought.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [greens.org.nz](https://www.greens.org.nz/climate_change_lens_on_major_government_decisions) - Labour-Greens Confidence and Supply Agreement 2017 (climate lens on government decisions)
- Evidence: [greens.org.nz](https://www.greens.org.nz/climate_change_lens_on_major_government_decisions)

### Increase uptake of zero-emission vehicles and introduce clean car standards as part of achieving the goals of the Zero Carbon Act.

**Greens** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered**  
`climate-greens-transport-decarbonisation`

> Clean car standard + ZEV uptake promised -> feebate live 1 Jul 2021, Clean Car Standard in force 2023

Both a feebate and a clean car standard were legislated and in force before the end of the term. That is what the agreement committed to.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [interest.co.nz](https://www.interest.co.nz/news/107748/proposed-cooperation-agreement-between-labour-and-greens-released-greens-delegates) - Labour-Greens Cooperation Agreement 2020, decarbonising public transport, increasing uptake of zero-emission vehicles, introducing clean car standards
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Land_Transport_(Clean_Vehicle_Discount_Scheme_Repeal)_Amendment_Act_2023)
- Evidence: [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/news/politics/521675/government-eases-too-stringent-clean-car-standard-rules)

### Support the switch of industrial process heat from coal and gas to renewable energy.

**Greens** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered**  
`climate-greens-industrial-process-heat`

> Renewable process heat support promised -> GIDI Partnerships $230m public + $860m private, approx 9.3 Mt lifetime abatement; coal boiler phase-out set for 2037

Both a large co-funding scheme and a regulatory phase-out were in place before the end of the term. The later reversal is outside the promising party's term.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [interest.co.nz](https://www.interest.co.nz/news/107748/proposed-cooperation-agreement-between-labour-and-greens-released-greens-delegates) - Labour-Greens Cooperation Agreement 2020, supporting the use of renewable energy for industrial heat
- Evidence: [eeca.govt.nz](https://www.eeca.govt.nz/co-funding-and-support/previously-funded-projects/approved-gidi-projects/)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/government-ban-new-coal-boilers-place)
- Evidence: [b2bnews.co.nz](https://b2bnews.co.nz/news/coal-boiler-ban-scrapped-what-it-means-for-nz-industry/)

### Pass a Zero Carbon Act and establish an independent Climate Commission.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered**  
`climate-labour-zero-carbon-act`

> Zero Carbon Act promised -> passed 13 Nov 2019, net zero (excl. biogenic methane) by 2050

The Act passed within the term. It created both the 2050 target and the independent Commission exactly as promised.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [mcguinnessinstitute.org](https://www.mcguinnessinstitute.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/WP202007-202009-AnalysisOfThe2017Labour-FINAL.pdf) - Labour-NZ First Coalition Agreement 2017, item 40 (also a Labour-Greens confidence and supply commitment)
- Evidence: [legislation.govt.nz](https://www.legislation.govt.nz/act/public/2019/0061/latest/LMS183848.html)
- Evidence: [climateactiontracker.org](https://climateactiontracker.org/countries/new-zealand/policies-action/)

### Achieve 100 percent renewable electricity generation by 2030, five years earlier than the previous 2035 goal. Support this with pumped hydro at Lake Onslow and a ban on new thermal baseload generation.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Partly delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`climate-labour-100-percent-renewable-2030`

> 100 percent renewable electricity by 2030 -> 87 percent of generation renewable in 2022

Renewable share rose, but the key enabling project was unfinished and the firm 2030 target was softened. The target date itself falls outside the term.

- Promise (manifesto): [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/news/political/425709/labour-promises-100-percent-renewable-electricity-generation-by-2030) - Labour 2020 election energy policy announcement (10 September 2020)
- Evidence: [mbie.govt.nz](https://www.mbie.govt.nz/about/news/energy-in-new-zealand-2023-shows-renewable-electricity-generation-increased-to-87-percent)
- Evidence: [mbie.govt.nz](https://www.mbie.govt.nz/building-and-energy/energy-and-natural-resources/low-emissions-economy/nz-battery)

### Run the Emissions Trading Scheme in line with the Zero Carbon Act's emissions budgets, including setting auction volumes and price controls in line with Climate Change Commission advice.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Partly delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`climate-labour-ets-settings-emissions-budgets`

> ETS settings aligned with budgets -> approx 35 million extra units released Dec 2022, all 4 auctions in 2023 failed, settings remade by Sept 2023

The settings were eventually aligned with Commission advice. That came only after a Cabinet decision that a court required to be remade, and a year of failed auctions.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [interest.co.nz](https://www.interest.co.nz/news/107748/proposed-cooperation-agreement-between-labour-and-greens-released-greens-delegates) - Labour-Greens Cooperation Agreement 2020: "achieving the purpose and goals of the Zero Carbon Act"
- Evidence: [lawyersforclimateaction.nz](https://www.lawyersforclimateaction.nz/news-events/ets-jr-win)
- Evidence: [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/news/country/580730/emissions-trading-scheme-year-s-final-auction-fails-to-sell-a-single-carbon-unit)

### If the Climate Commission decides agriculture should be included in the Emissions Trading Scheme (ETS), free allocation to agriculture on entry will be 95 percent. All revenue will be recycled back into agriculture for innovation, mitigation and additional forestry planting.

**NZ First** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Superseded**  
`climate-nzf-agriculture-ets-95-percent`

> 95 percent free allocation on ETS entry promised -> agriculture never entered the ETS

Agriculture never entered the ETS during the term. The 95 percent free allocation and revenue recycling arrangement never took effect.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [mcguinnessinstitute.org](https://www.mcguinnessinstitute.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/WP202007-202009-AnalysisOfThe2017Labour-FINAL.pdf) - Labour-NZ First Coalition Agreement 2017, item 41
- Evidence: [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/news/country/476451/he-waka-eke-noa-government-proposes-farm-level-system-by-2025)
- Evidence: [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/news/political/519248/coalition-exempts-farmers-from-ets-sets-up-fresh-working-group)

### Plant 100 million trees a year under a One Billion Trees programme, reaching one billion trees by 2028.

**NZ First** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Partly delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`climate-nzf-billion-trees`

> 1,000,000,000 trees by 2028 (100m/yr) -> approx 149.4 million recorded planted by May 2020

A programme was established and large-scale planting occurred, but the run rate was roughly half what a billion trees by 2028 required.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [mcguinnessinstitute.org](https://www.mcguinnessinstitute.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/WP202007-202009-AnalysisOfThe2017Labour-FINAL.pdf) - Labour-NZ First Coalition Agreement 2017, item 1b: "Planting 100 million trees per year in a Billion Trees Planting Programme"
- Evidence: [mcguinnessinstitute.org](https://www.mcguinnessinstitute.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/WP202007-202009-AnalysisOfThe2017Labour-FINAL.pdf)
- Evidence: [climatecommission.govt.nz](https://www.climatecommission.govt.nz/our-work/monitoring/emissions-reduction-monitoring/erm-2025)

### Make the Government's vehicle fleet, where practicable, emissions-free by 2025/26.

**NZ First** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`climate-nzf-government-fleet-emissions-free`

> Emissions-free fleet by 2025/26 -> 78 of 15,473 government vehicles electric at Oct 2019, target later redefined

Some electrification occurred and a programme was created, but the target was redefined from an emissions-free fleet to emissions-free new purchases.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [mcguinnessinstitute.org](https://www.mcguinnessinstitute.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/WP202007-202009-AnalysisOfThe2017Labour-FINAL.pdf) - Labour-NZ First Coalition Agreement 2017, item 39
- Evidence: [mcguinnessinstitute.org](https://www.mcguinnessinstitute.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/WP202007-202009-AnalysisOfThe2017Labour-FINAL.pdf)

### Keep a split-gas approach to methane and carbon dioxide through to 2050. Review the methane science and targets in 2024 for consistency with no additional warming from agricultural methane emissions.

**ACT** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Largely delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`climate-act-methane-target-review`

> Methane target reviewed for no additional warming -> target cut from 24-47% to 14-24% below 2017 levels by 2050

The split-gas approach was maintained, and the review was carried out and acted on. The decision came in October 2025 rather than in 2024.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/actnz/mailings/6945/attachments/original/National_ACT_Agreement.pdf) - National-ACT Coalition Agreement 2023, maintain a split-gas approach and review the methane science and targets in 2024 for consistency with no additional warming
- Evidence: [thespinoff.co.nz](https://thespinoff.co.nz/politics/14-10-2025/the-new-lowered-methane-targets-explained)
- Evidence: [sciencemediacentre.co.nz](https://www.sciencemediacentre.co.nz/2025/10/13/nzs-new-targets-to-manage-its-methane-emissions-expert-reaction/)
- Evidence: [climatecommission.govt.nz](https://www.climatecommission.govt.nz/our-work/advice-to-government-topic/review-of-the-2050-emissions-target/2024-review-of-the-2050-emissions-target/final-report/executive-summary-2050-target-and-isa)

### Repeal the ban on offshore oil and gas exploration.

**ACT** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`climate-act-repeal-offshore-oil-gas-ban`

> Repeal 2018 offshore exploration ban -> repealed by Crown Minerals Amendment Act 2025, royal assent 5 Aug 2025

The ban was repealed by Act of Parliament within the term.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/actnz/mailings/6945/attachments/original/National_ACT_Agreement.pdf) - National-ACT Coalition Agreement 2023: "Repeal the ban on offshore oil and gas exploration"
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crown_Minerals_Amendment_Act_2025)
- Evidence: [nzpam.govt.nz](https://www.nzpam.govt.nz/nz-industry/crown-minerals-act-law-changes)

### Let farmers and landowners offset on-farm sequestration (such as riparian planting, shelter belts and scattered vegetation) against their on-farm emissions.

**ACT** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Not delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`climate-act-on-farm-sequestration-offsets`

> On-farm sequestration offsetting -> no mechanism in place; on-farm pricing abandoned in Jan 2026

No on-farm offsetting mechanism was created. The decision not to price farm emissions removed the framework it would have operated in.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/actnz/mailings/6945/attachments/original/National_ACT_Agreement.pdf) - National-ACT Coalition Agreement 2023: "Enable farmers and landowners to offset sequestration against their on-farm emissions"
- Evidence: [environment.govt.nz](https://environment.govt.nz/assets/publications/climate-change/ERP2/New-Zealands-second-emissions-reduction-plan-Amended-January-2026.pdf)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/agriculture-come-out-ets)

### Stop the previous government's review of the Emissions Trading Scheme, to restore confidence and certainty to the carbon market.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Partly delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`climate-nzf-stop-ets-review`

> Stop ETS review to restore certainty -> review stopped; all 4 auctions in 2025 failed to sell a single unit

The review was stopped as promised. On the auction evidence, the stated purpose of restoring carbon-market confidence and certainty has not been achieved.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/nzfirst/pages/4462/attachments/original/National___NZF_Coalition_Agreement_signed_-_24_Nov_2023.pdf) - National-NZ First Coalition Agreement 2023: "Stop the current review of the ETS system to restore confidence and certainty"
- Evidence: [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/news/country/580730/emissions-trading-scheme-year-s-final-auction-fails-to-sell-a-single-carbon-unit)
- Evidence: [climatecommission.govt.nz](https://www.climatecommission.govt.nz/our-work/monitoring/emissions-reduction-monitoring/erm-2025)

### Encourage the uptake of on-farm emissions reduction mitigations, such as low-methane genetics and low-methane animal feed, rather than pricing farm emissions.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`climate-nzf-methane-mitigation-incentives`

> Incentivise low-methane technology -> $400m over 4 years plus $50.5m over 5 years committed

Substantial research and commercialisation funding was delivered, but uptake incentives have not yet produced measurable on-farm methane reductions.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/nzfirst/pages/4462/attachments/original/National___NZF_Coalition_Agreement_signed_-_24_Nov_2023.pdf) - National-NZ First Coalition Agreement 2023, incentivise uptake of emissions reduction mitigations such as low methane genetics and low methane feed
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/agriculture-come-out-ets)
- Evidence: [climatecommission.govt.nz](https://www.climatecommission.govt.nz/our-work/monitoring/emissions-reduction-monitoring/erm-2025)

### Deliver net zero by 2050, including by doubling New Zealand's renewable electricity generation.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **In progress** &middot; _contested call_  
`climate-national-net-zero-double-renewables`

> Net zero 2050 + double renewables -> EB1 284.1/290 Mt and EB2 303.1/305 Mt projected met, EB3 projected 249.2 Mt vs 240 Mt budget

The 2050 target is intact and the first two budgets are on track. The third budget is projected to be missed, and the term is not finished.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/nzfirst/pages/4462/attachments/original/National___NZF_Coalition_Agreement_signed_-_24_Nov_2023.pdf) - National-NZ First Coalition Agreement 2023: "Deliver Net Zero by 2050 including by doubling New Zealand's renewable electricity"
- Evidence: [climatecommission.govt.nz](https://www.climatecommission.govt.nz/our-work/monitoring/emissions-reduction-monitoring/erm-2025)
- Evidence: [environment.govt.nz](https://environment.govt.nz/assets/publications/climate-change/ERP2/New-Zealands-second-emissions-reduction-plan-Amended-January-2026.pdf)

### Scrap the Clean Car Discount, including the fee on higher-emitting vehicles that National labelled the "ute tax".

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`climate-national-end-clean-car-discount`

> Clean Car Discount scrapped -> ended 31 Dec 2023 after approx $579m rebates paid vs approx $290m fees collected

Repealed by legislation within the first month of government, exactly as promised.

- Promise (manifesto): [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/government-ends-%E2%80%98ute-tax%E2%80%99) - National 2023 campaign commitment to scrap the Clean Car Discount / "ute tax"
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Land_Transport_(Clean_Vehicle_Discount_Scheme_Repeal)_Amendment_Act_2023)
- Evidence: [nzta.govt.nz](https://www.nzta.govt.nz/vehicles/clean-car-programme/clean-car-discount-ended-on-31-december-2023)

### Deliver 10,000 public EV chargers by 2030, backed by $257 million over four years using a co-investment model modelled on Ultra-Fast Broadband.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Not delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`climate-national-10000-ev-chargers`

> 10,000 public chargers by 2030 ($257m) -> 0 delivered under the programme and 0 contracts signed as at March 2026

No chargers had been delivered and no contracts signed at 30 months. The required build rate is now far above the current national rate.

- Promise (manifesto): [national.org.nz](https://www.national.org.nz/policies/supercharging-ev-infrastructure) - National 2023 policy. Supercharging EV Infrastructure
- Evidence: [b2bnews.co.nz](https://b2bnews.co.nz/news/nz-ev-charger-rollout-stalls-30-months-zero-contracts-signed/)
- Evidence: [environment.govt.nz](https://environment.govt.nz/assets/publications/climate-change/ERP2/New-Zealands-second-emissions-reduction-plan-Amended-January-2026.pdf)

### Keep agriculture out of the Emissions Trading Scheme and remove the legislated 2025 backstop that would have brought farm emissions into it.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`climate-national-agriculture-out-of-ets`

> Agriculture out of the ETS -> legislated before 1 Jan 2025; $400m over 4 years for mitigation technology

Agriculture was legislated out of the ETS within the promised timeframe and the backstop removed.

- Promise (manifesto): [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/news/political/519248/coalition-exempts-farmers-from-ets-sets-up-fresh-working-group) - National 2023 election commitment to keep agriculture out of the ETS
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/agriculture-come-out-ets)
- Evidence: [environment.govt.nz](https://environment.govt.nz/assets/publications/climate-change/ERP2/New-Zealands-second-emissions-reduction-plan-Amended-January-2026.pdf)

## Environment & Conservation

_Freshwater rules, indigenous biodiversity protection, marine sanctuaries, waste policy and the consenting system were rebuilt under the 2017-2023 Labour-led government and then substantially unwound or redirected by the National-led coalition from 2023, making this one of the sharpest policy reversals of the two eras._

### Work to achieve the outcomes of Te Mana o te Taiao, the Aotearoa New Zealand Biodiversity Strategy 2020. Protect kauri, build on pest management programmes, and act to minimise waste and problem plastics.

**Greens** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Partly delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`environment-greens-biodiversity-waste`

> Container return scheme with 20c deposit -> proposed March 2022, dropped 2023 without legislation

Strategy documents, pest programmes and kerbside recycling reform were delivered. But the flagship waste measure, the container return scheme, was abandoned, and the biodiversity strategy carried no binding targets.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [interest.co.nz](https://www.interest.co.nz/news/107748/proposed-cooperation-agreement-between-labour-and-greens-released-greens-delegates) - Labour-Greens Cooperation Agreement 2020, protecting our environment and biodiversity through Te Mana o te Taiao, protecting kauri, building on pest management programmes, and taking action to minimise waste and problem plastics
- Evidence: [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/news/national/463243/government-plans-to-shake-up-new-zealand-s-inadequate-waste-recycling)

- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Predator_Free_2050)

### Minimise waste to landfill, with significant reductions achieved by 2020.

**Greens** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Partly delivered**

Two substantial waste measures were delivered. But the levy reform arrived after the agreement's 2020 deadline, and landfill volumes did not fall by then.

## Transport & Infrastructure

_Between 2017 and 2026 New Zealand's two governments pulled land transport policy in opposite directions (one towards rail, light rail and mode shift, the other back towards state highways and road pricing) while both struggled to convert announced megaprojects into built infrastructure._

### Work will begin on light rail from the city to the airport in Auckland.

**Greens** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Partly delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`transport-labour-greens-light-rail-airport`

> Work to begin on city-to-airport light rail -> planning only, approx $228m spent, cancelled Jan 2024

Planning work did begin, as the clause required, but no line was built and the project was cancelled.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [thestandard.org.nz](https://thestandard.org.nz/labour-green-confidence-and-supply-agreement-released/) - Labour-Greens confidence & supply agreement 2017
- Evidence: [greaterauckland.org.nz](https://www.greaterauckland.org.nz/2024/01/15/light-rail-officially-dies/)
- Evidence: [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/news/politics/506674/national-led-government-officially-cancels-auckland-light-rail-plans)

### Auckland's East-West motorway link will not proceed as currently proposed.

**Greens** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered**  
`transport-labour-greens-east-west-link`

The project as proposed did not proceed at any point in the 2017-2023 period, which is what the clause committed to.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [thestandard.org.nz](https://thestandard.org.nz/labour-green-confidence-and-supply-agreement-released/) - Labour-Greens confidence & supply agreement 2017
- Evidence: [transport.govt.nz](https://www.transport.govt.nz/assets/Uploads/Government-Policy-Statement-on-land-transport-2024-FINAL.pdf)
- Evidence: [1news.co.nz](https://www.1news.co.nz/2026/07/09/govt-pulls-flagship-roading-projects-into-the-slow-lane/)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/transport-pipeline-provides-certainty)

### Create a $1.5 billion Cycle Super Highway fund for protected cycleways in major cities, plus more than $400 million for local walking and cycling improvements, within a $13.6 billion decade-long transport plan.

**Greens** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Not delivered**  
`transport-labour-greens-cycle-highways`

> $1.5b cycle super highway fund promised -> no such fund created; walking and cycling class set at $70m-$110m a year in GPS 2024

The specific $1.5b fund was never created, and cycling funding stayed an order of magnitude smaller.

- Evidence: [transport.govt.nz](https://www.transport.govt.nz/assets/Uploads/Government-Policy-Statement-on-land-transport-2024-FINAL.pdf)
- Evidence: [nzta.govt.nz](https://nzta.govt.nz/planning-and-investment/national-land-transport-programme/what-guides-the-nltp/government-policy-statement-on-land-transport)

Targets were set and a funding mechanism established, but only a small share of the fleet had been converted by the handover.

### Deliver $6.8 billion of transport projects under the $12b New Zealand Upgrade Programme, including Penlink (open late 2025), Mill Road (complete 2027/28), Papakura to Drury South (complete late 2025), Drury rail stations (complete late 2024) and the Northern Pathway (complete 2023/24).

### Reprioritise National Land Transport Fund spending away from new state highways and towards rail infrastructure in cities and regions, public transport, and walking and cycling.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Largely delivered**  
`transport-labour-gps-rebalance`

The funding rebalance and the new rail funding architecture happened. But walking and cycling stayed a minor share, and it was reversed within months of the handover.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [thestandard.org.nz](https://thestandard.org.nz/labour-green-confidence-and-supply-agreement-released/) - Labour-NZ First coalition agreement 2017 and Labour-Greens confidence & supply agreement 2017 (transport clauses)

### Deliver the Let's Get Wellington Moving programme (mass rapid transit, a second Mt Victoria tunnel, Basin Reserve improvements and the Golden Mile), jointly funded by the Crown (60 per cent) and Wellington councils (40 per cent).

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Not delivered**  
`transport-labour-lgwm`

### Commission a feasibility study on the options for moving the Ports of Auckland, with Northport at Whangarei given serious consideration.

**NZ First** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`transport-labour-nzfirst-ports-auckland`

> Feasibility study on moving Ports of Auckland -> study delivered, relocation costed at $10.3b, no decision by Oct 2023

The study NZ First bargained for was delivered, but the relocation it was meant to enable never got a decision.

### Reverse the blanket speed limit reductions made under the previous government, where it is safe to do so.

**ACT** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`transport-national-act-speed-limits`

> Reverse blanket speed limit reductions -> 38 state highway sections reverted automatically, 49 more consulted; completed by 1 July 2025

The rule was made and the reversals were completed on the stated deadline.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [scoop.co.nz](https://www.scoop.co.nz/stories/PA2501/S00083/speed-limit-reduction-reversals-begin.htm) - National-ACT coalition agreement 2023

### Run a public tipline so people can report excessive road cone use, and cut over-the-top temporary traffic management.

**ACT** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Largely delivered**  
`transport-national-act-road-cones`

> Road cone tipline -> 1,100+ reports in 5 months, excessive cones at 6 per cent of sites, pilot ended early; NZTA traffic management spend down approx $46m

The tipline ran and the wider traffic management reform produced measurable savings, but the tipline itself was wound up early after finding limited over-coning.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [1news.co.nz](https://www.1news.co.nz/2025/06/10/hundreds-report-excessive-road-cones-in-first-week-of-new-hotline/) - Government action plan commitment delivered by the ACT Minister for Workplace Relations and Safety. WorkSafe road cone tipline, announced June 2025

This would reuse Wellington's existing marine infrastructure and build a new dual-level wharf at Picton, at a cost far below the cancelled programme.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **In progress** &middot; _contested call_

### Build 13 new Roads of National Significance, including four-laning State Highway 1 from Whangarei to Tauranga, Mill Road, a second Mt Victoria Tunnel, the Hope Bypass, the Woodend Bypass, Hamilton Southern Links and Petone to Grenada, as part of a transport plan costed at about $24 billion.

## Energy

_New Zealand's energy debate shifted from how fast to reach a fully renewable electricity system to how to keep the lights on affordably as domestic gas ran down, with each government reversing the other's central project._

### Request the Climate Commission to plan the transition to 100 per cent renewable electricity by 2035, including geothermal, in a normal hydrological year.

**Greens** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Partly delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`energy-labour-greens-renewable-2035-plan`

> Plan for 100 per cent renewable electricity by 2035 -> no binding pathway adopted; 88.1 per cent renewable in 2023

The Commission was created and did advise on electricity. But no 100 per cent transition plan with a defined pathway was adopted.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [thestandard.org.nz](https://thestandard.org.nz/labour-green-confidence-and-supply-agreement-released/) - Labour-Greens confidence & supply agreement 2017
- Evidence: [axios.com](https://www.axios.com/new-zealand-renewable-energy-2030-36493cd8-ff1a-4577-9b51-f2ca43d945a8.html)
- Evidence: [mbie.govt.nz](https://www.mbie.govt.nz/building-and-energy/energy-and-natural-resources/energy-statistics-and-modelling/energy-publications-and-technical-papers/energy-in-new-zealand/energy-in-new-zealand-2025/electricity)

### End energy poverty and ensure everyone has a warm, dry home, including through a large-scale programme of home insulation and heating retrofits for low-income owner-occupiers.

**Greens** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Largely delivered**  
`gapfill-greens-warmer-kiwi-homes`

> 26,500 retrofits a year -> 122,589 retrofits delivered 2018 to August 2023 (81,920 insulation, 40,669 heating)

The retrofit programme ran at or above its annual target for the whole term and was expanded. The broader goal of ending energy poverty was not achieved.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [thestandard.org.nz](https://thestandard.org.nz/labour-green-confidence-and-supply-agreement-released/) - Labour-Green Confidence and Supply Agreement 2017, policy goal 13, end energy poverty and ensure everyone has warm, dry homes
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/over-50000-more-kiwis-be-eligible-insulation-grants)
- Evidence: [eeca.govt.nz](https://www.eeca.govt.nz/co-funding-and-support/products/warmer-kiwi-homes-programme/)

### Achieve 100 per cent renewable electricity generation by 2030 in a normal hydrological year, bringing forward the previous 2035 goal by five years.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Partly delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`energy-labour-100pc-renewable-2030`

> 100 per cent renewable electricity by 2030 -> 88.1 per cent in 2023, 85.5 per cent in 2024

The renewable share rose materially during the two terms. But no settled pathway to 100 per cent existed at the handover, and the enabling storage project was never decided.

- Promise (manifesto): [axios.com](https://www.axios.com/new-zealand-renewable-energy-2030-36493cd8-ff1a-4577-9b51-f2ca43d945a8.html) - Labour 2020 campaign energy policy, announced 17 September 2020
- Evidence: [mbie.govt.nz](https://www.mbie.govt.nz/building-and-energy/energy-and-natural-resources/energy-statistics-and-modelling/energy-publications-and-technical-papers/energy-in-new-zealand/energy-in-new-zealand-2025/electricity)
- Evidence: [mbie.govt.nz](https://www.mbie.govt.nz/building-and-energy/energy-and-natural-resources/energy-statistics-and-modelling/energy-publications-and-technical-papers/energy-in-new-zealand/energy-in-new-zealand-2025/renewables)
- Evidence: [mbie.govt.nz](https://www.mbie.govt.nz/about/news/favourable-hydro-conditions-see-record-high-share-of-electricity-generation-from-renewables)

### Spend $70 million investigating a pumped hydro scheme at Lake Onslow (the NZ Battery project) to solve the dry-year problem, and decide whether to build it.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`energy-labour-nz-battery-onslow`

> $70m investigation plus a build decision -> studies delivered, no build decision, investigations stopped late 2023

The investigation the promise funded did take place. But the decision it was supposed to lead to was never made before the term ended.

- Promise (manifesto): [axios.com](https://www.axios.com/new-zealand-renewable-energy-2030-36493cd8-ff1a-4577-9b51-f2ca43d945a8.html) - Labour 2020 campaign energy policy. NZ Battery / Lake Onslow investigation
- Evidence: [mbie.govt.nz](https://www.mbie.govt.nz/building-and-energy/energy-and-natural-resources/low-emissions-economy/nz-battery)
- Evidence: [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/news/national/503816/govt-confirms-it-is-dumping-hugely-wasteful-lake-onslow-battery-project)

### Introduce a Winter Energy Payment of up to $700 a year for couples and people with dependants, and $450 for single people. Pay it automatically to superannuitants and main benefit recipients over the colder months, starting 1 July 2018.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered**  
`energy-labour-winter-energy-payment`

> $450 single / $700 couples from 1 July 2018 -> delivered on time to over 1 million people

Introduced on the promised date at the promised rates to the promised group.

### Investigate the feasibility of reopening the Marsden Point oil refinery as part of a fuel security study.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`energy-national-nzfirst-marsden-point`

The commitment as written was to investigate, and the investigation was commissioned and completed.

`energy-national-nzfirst-fuel-security-plan`

The plan was published and then put to use, though about two years after the commitment was signed.

### Reverse the 2018 ban on new offshore oil and gas exploration outside onshore Taranaki, with legislation passed by the end of 2024.

`energy-national-oil-gas-ban-reversal`

The ban was reversed as promised but roughly seven months late, and only one exploration permit had resulted a year on.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/government-reverse-oil-and-gas-exploration-ban) - Coalition agreement commitment to reverse the offshore oil and gas exploration ban; legislation promised by the end of 2024

- Evidence: [newswire.co.nz](https://newswire.co.nz/2026/07/first-offshore-oil-gas-permit-since-ban-reversal-taranaki/)
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crown_Minerals_Amendment_Act_2025)
- Evidence: [thediplomat.com](https://thediplomat.com/2025/06/new-zealand-reverses-offshore-oil-and-gas-exploration-ban-amid-energy-security-concerns/)

### Review the performance of the electricity market and improve its regulation so competition works and power is affordable and reliable.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Largely delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`energy-national-electricity-market-review`

> Electricity market review -> report Oct 2025, structural separation rejected, hedge access rules from 1 July 2026, penalties from $2m to $10m in 2027

The review was completed and binding conduct rules took effect. But the enforcement regime that gives them teeth does not arrive until 2027.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/urgent-action-taken-bolster-energy-security) - Government energy security package announced 26 August 2024, improve electricity market regulation and review market performance
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/delivering-fairer-electricity-market-kiwi-consumers)
- Evidence: [ea.govt.nz](https://www.ea.govt.nz/news/press-release/electricity-authority-welcomes-outcome-of-electricity-market-review/)
- Evidence: [comcom.govt.nz](https://www.comcom.govt.nz/news-and-media/news-and-events/2025/electricity-authority-moves-to-level-the-power-playing-field/)

### Remove the regulatory barriers to building a liquefied natural gas import facility so New Zealand can import gas when domestic supply is short.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Largely delivered**  
`energy-national-lng-import`

> LNG import facility -> at contracting stage Aug 2026, target operation 2027 to early 2028

Regulatory barriers were addressed and a facility is at contracting stage, but nothing is built and the term is not finished.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/urgent-action-taken-bolster-energy-security) - Government energy security package announced 26 August 2024, remove regulatory barriers to LNG imports
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/delivering-lng-support-energy-security)

### Establish a $200 million co-investment fund and take Crown cornerstone stakes in new gas field developments, to reduce sovereign risk and lift domestic gas supply.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`energy-national-gas-co-investment`

> $200m gas co-investment fund -> fund created May 2025 and widened Nov 2025; gas reserves still fell 23 per cent to 731 PJ by Jan 2026

The fund exists and was broadened, but no completed investment or reversal of the gas production decline is yet evident.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [nzherald.co.nz](https://www.nzherald.co.nz/bay-of-plenty-times/news/budget-2025-energy-sector-welcomes-200m-government-gas-exploration-fund/MMB5AO5JFFGDJG44QNEYGYFF6M/) - Budget 2025 gas co-investment fund and Crown cornerstone stake policy
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/widened-scope-co-investment-new-gas)
- Evidence: [newswire.co.nz](https://newswire.co.nz/2026/07/first-offshore-oil-gas-permit-since-ban-reversal-taranaki/)

### Make it faster and cheaper to consent renewable electricity generation and network infrastructure. Do this through a one-stop-shop fast-track approvals regime and new national direction under the Resource Management Act.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`energy-national-fast-track-renewables`

> Faster consenting for renewables -> 6 renewable projects approved under fast-track by July 2026, including a 380MW wind farm

The regime was legislated, the national direction was made, and six renewable projects had been consented through it by mid-2026.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/electrifying-growth-infrastructure-and-energy-rma-national-direction-changes) - Coalition Government fast-track consenting programme and RMA national direction for infrastructure and renewable electricity
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/fast-track-approved-project-could-deliver-new-zealand%E2%80%99s-largest-wind-farm)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/sixth-renewable-energy-project-fast-tracked)
- Evidence: [fasttrack.govt.nz](https://www.fasttrack.govt.nz/projects/southland-wind-farm)

### Take urgent action to secure electricity supply and manage dry-year risk after the 2024 gas shortage and wholesale price spike. This includes easing restrictions on lines companies owning generation and securing gentailer access to hydro contingent storage.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`energy-national-dry-year-security`

> Secure supply after the 2024 crisis -> wholesale prices peaked above $800/MWh in Aug 2024; Crown put up to $200m into Genesis in Feb 2026

Specific measures were implemented and the immediate crisis eased. But structural dry-year and gas-decline risk remains unresolved, with the LNG facility and new gas investment still pending.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/urgent-action-taken-bolster-energy-security) - Government energy security package announced 26 August 2024
- Evidence: [ea.govt.nz](https://www.ea.govt.nz/news/eye-on-electricity/what-was-behind-high-wholesale-electricity-prices/)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/government-invests-support-energy-security)
- Evidence: [mbie.govt.nz](https://www.mbie.govt.nz/building-and-energy/energy-and-natural-resources/energy-statistics-and-modelling/energy-publications-and-technical-papers/energy-in-new-zealand/energy-in-new-zealand-2025/electricity)

## Child Poverty & Welfare

_Labour came to office promising to legislate child poverty targets and lift incomes for families and beneficiaries, while the 2023 coalition campaigned on tightening benefit obligations, indexing benefits to prices instead of wages and moving people off Jobseeker Support into work._

### Overhaul the welfare system, ensure access to entitlements, remove excessive sanctions and review Working for Families. The aim is that everyone has a standard of living and income that enables them to live in dignity and participate in their communities, and that lifts children and their families out of poverty.

**Greens** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`welfare-greens-welfare-overhaul`

> Remove excessive sanctions -> sanctions applied fell from 60,588 in 2017 to 25,329 in 2023

Sanctions fell and indexation was made more generous. But the promised system overhaul and Working for Families rewrite did not happen within the two terms.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [interest.co.nz](https://www.interest.co.nz/sites/default/files/embedded_images/Labour%20and%20Green%20Party%20Confidence%20and%20Supply%20Agreement.pdf) - Labour-Green Confidence and Supply Agreement 2017
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/government-begins-reset-welfare-system)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/cost-living-support-beneficiary-households)

### Cancel National's legislated April 2018 tax threshold cuts and replace them with a Families Package. It included a Best Start payment of $60 a week for every child under one, extended to age three for low and middle income families and reaching nearly 60,000 families a year, a higher Family Tax Credit, and Working for Families extended to 30,000 more families.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered**  
`welfare-labour-families-package-best-start`

> Best Start $60 a week per child under three -> legislated Dec 2017, first payments 1 July 2018

Legislated within weeks of taking office and paid at the promised rates and dates.

- Promise (manifesto): [img.scoop.co.nz](https://img.scoop.co.nz/media/pdfs/1707/Families_Package_factsheet.pdf) - Labour 2017 Families Package campaign policy factsheet
- Evidence: [msd.govt.nz](https://www.msd.govt.nz/about-msd-and-our-work/newsroom/2017/families-package-qas.html)
- Evidence: [interest.co.nz](https://www.interest.co.nz/news/88733/families-young-children-and-pensioners-main-beneficiaries-labours-families-package)
- Evidence: [treasury.govt.nz](https://www.treasury.govt.nz/publications/information-release/families-package-information-release)

### Pay a Winter Energy Payment of $450 a year to single superannuitants and beneficiaries and $700 a year to couples and people with dependent children, automatically and without application, reaching about one million New Zealanders.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered**  
`welfare-labour-winter-energy-payment`

> $450 single / $700 couples, about 1 million people -> paid from 1 July 2018, $1.81b over four years

Paid at the promised rates, to the promised group, from the promised date, and still in place.

- Promise (manifesto): [img.scoop.co.nz](https://img.scoop.co.nz/media/pdfs/1707/Winter_Energy_Payment_factsheet.pdf) - Labour 2017 Winter Energy Payment factsheet
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/winter-energy-payments-keep-million-kiwis-warm-and-healthy)
- Evidence: [msd.govt.nz](https://www.msd.govt.nz/about-msd-and-our-work/newsroom/2017/families-package-qas.html)
- Evidence: [motu.nz](https://www.motu.nz/our-research/population-and-labour/individual-and-group-outcomes/impact-winter-energy-payment)

### Raise the Working for Families in-work tax credit by $25 a week from 1 April 2024, and lift the Working for Families abatement threshold to $50,000 from 1 April 2026.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Not delivered**  
`welfare-labour-wff-2023`

> In-work tax credit +$25 a week from 1 April 2024 -> not delivered by Labour; legislated by National from 31 July 2024

The promising party left office before implementing any part of it, though its successor later legislated the in-work tax credit component.

- Promise (manifesto): [labour.org.nz](https://www.labour.org.nz/news-release_cost_living_gst) - Labour 2023 campaign policy announcement, 13 August 2023
- Evidence: [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/news/election-2023/495698/labour-promises-to-remove-gst-from-fruit-and-vegetables-boost-working-for-families)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/responsible-tax-relief-squeezed-middle)

### Develop a social unemployment insurance scheme. Workers who lose their job would keep about 80 per cent of their income for a period while they look for work or retrain.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Abandoned**  
`gapfill-national-labour-income-insurance-scheme`

> 80 per cent income replacement promised -> scheme shelved 8 February 2023, never established

The promising party explicitly shelved the scheme in February 2023, and it was never revived before the end of the term.

- Promise (manifesto): [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/building-stronger-support-workers-post-covid) - Labour manifesto commitment on social unemployment insurance, as cited by Finance Minister Grant Robertson on 20 May 2021
- Evidence: [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/news/political/483875/watch-tvnz-rnz-merger-scrapped-income-insurance-and-hate-speech-laws-delayed)
- Evidence: [mbie.govt.nz](https://www.mbie.govt.nz/have-your-say/income-insurance)
- Evidence: [nzherald.co.nz](https://www.nzherald.co.nz/business/personal-finance/revealed-the-huge-cost-of-the-governments-shelved-income-insurance-scheme/PCCIWVHBDVDSDIKT2VJHBV6ZHI/)

### Keep the age of eligibility for New Zealand Superannuation at 65.

**NZ First** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered**  
`welfare-nzf-super-age-65-2017`

> Superannuation age 65 -> unchanged at 65 across both terms

The age stayed at 65 for the whole term, which is exactly what the commitment required.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [mcguinnessinstitute.org](https://www.mcguinnessinstitute.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/WP202007-202009-AnalysisOfThe2017Labour-FINAL.pdf) - Labour-NZ First Coalition Agreement 2017, superannuation eligibility age
- Evidence: [mcguinnessinstitute.org](https://www.mcguinnessinstitute.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/WP202007-202009-AnalysisOfThe2017Labour-FINAL.pdf)
- Evidence: [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/nzfirst/pages/4462/attachments/original/1700784896/National___NZF_Coalition_Agreement_signed_-_24_Nov_2023.pdf)

### Introduce a SuperGold smartcard with expanded discounts and concessions for cardholders.

**NZ First** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Largely delivered**  
`welfare-nzf-supergold-smartcard-2017`

> SuperGold smartcard -> website and app launched Oct 2019 covering about 5,500 businesses

The discount network and digital platform were delivered, but the smartcard itself did not eventuate within the term.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [mcguinnessinstitute.org](https://www.mcguinnessinstitute.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/WP202007-202009-AnalysisOfThe2017Labour-FINAL.pdf) - Labour-NZ First Coalition Agreement 2017, SuperGold smartcard
- Evidence: [mcguinnessinstitute.org](https://www.mcguinnessinstitute.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/WP202007-202009-AnalysisOfThe2017Labour-FINAL.pdf)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/supergold-card-being-upgraded)

### Implement sanctions, including electronic money management, for beneficiaries who can work but refuse to take agreed steps to find a job.

**ACT** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`welfare-act-money-management-sanctions`

> Electronic money management sanction -> in force from early 2025; about 3,700 sanctions applied in June 2024

Electronic money management was legislated and is operating as a sanction, as the agreement specified.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/actnz/mailings/6945/attachments/original/National_ACT_Agreement.pdf) - National-ACT Coalition Agreement 2023
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/green-light-welfare-works)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/welfare-sanctions-expand-job-searches-upskilling)

### Make two years the lifetime entitlement for the JobSeeker WorkReady benefit, a change the party said would affect roughly 99,000 people.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Superseded**  
`welfare-nzf-jobseeker-two-year-limit`

> Two-year lifetime Jobseeker entitlement -> reduced in the coalition agreement to 'consider time limits for under 25s'

The manifesto's hard two-year lifetime cap was replaced in the coalition agreement by a weaker commitment to consider time limits for under-25s only.

- Promise (manifesto): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/nzfirst/pages/4158/attachments/original/1696631089/2023-New-Zealand-First-Policy-Manifesto-1.pdf) - New Zealand First 2023 Policy Manifesto
- Evidence: [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/nzfirst/pages/4462/attachments/original/1700784896/National___NZF_Coalition_Agreement_signed_-_24_Nov_2023.pdf)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/green-light-welfare-works)

### Strengthen obligations on Jobseeker work ready beneficiaries to find work, make use of sanctions for non-compliance with work obligations, and consider time limits for under 25s.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Largely delivered**  
`welfare-nzf-jobseeker-obligations`

> Stronger obligations and sanctions -> delivered from Aug 2024; no under-25 time limit as at Aug 2026

The obligations and sanctions half of the commitment was fully delivered. The time-limit element was only ever to be 'considered', and it has not produced a policy change.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/nzfirst/pages/4462/attachments/original/1700784896/National___NZF_Coalition_Agreement_signed_-_24_Nov_2023.pdf) - National-NZ First Coalition Agreement 2023
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/green-light-welfare-works)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/welfare-sanctions-expand-job-searches-upskilling)

### Give SuperGold Card holders who own and live in their only home a 50 per cent local authority rates rebate of up to $1,600 a year. Also give them two free doctor's visits a year, including an annual eye test.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`welfare-nzf-supergold-rates-rebate`

> 50 percent rates rebate up to $1,600 -> maximum rebate lifted to $805 with a $45,000 threshold from 1 July 2025

Rates relief for SuperGold holders was materially expanded, but at roughly half the promised cap and on a different basis, and the free GP visits did not happen.

- Promise (manifesto): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/nzfirst/pages/4158/attachments/original/1696631089/2023-New-Zealand-First-Policy-Manifesto-1.pdf) - New Zealand First 2023 Policy Manifesto
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/rates-relief-66000-more-supergold-cardholders)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/rate-rebate-scheme-assist-ratepayers)

### Upgrade the SuperGold Card and Veterans Card to maximise their potential benefit for all cardholders.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`welfare-nzf-supergold-upgrade`

> Upgrade SuperGold Card -> $42.9m committed May 2026, card rollout scheduled to start Oct 2028

The upgrade has been designed and funded, but does not reach cardholders until October 2028, well beyond the term in which it was promised.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/nzfirst/pages/4462/attachments/original/1700784896/National___NZF_Coalition_Agreement_signed_-_24_Nov_2023.pdf) - National-NZ First Coalition Agreement 2023
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/supergold-card-being-upgraded)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/supergold-information-hub-live)

### Index main benefit increases to the consumer price index rather than to net average wage growth.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`welfare-national-cpi-indexation`

> CPI indexation restored -> legislated Feb 2024, $669.5m saved across the forecast period to 2027/28

Legislated under urgency within three months of taking office and applied from the first annual adjustment.

- Promise (manifesto): [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/programmes/news-extras/story/2018902030/election-2023-rnz-s-guide-to-party-policy) - National 2023 welfare policy, as summarised in RNZ's guide to party policy
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/cost-living-support-beneficiary-households)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/cost-living-support-over-14-million-kiwis)

### Introduce a traffic light system for Jobseeker Support beneficiaries, with benefit reductions or community work after repeated failures to meet obligations. Add job check-ins and contracted job coaching for under-25s.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`welfare-national-traffic-light-sanctions`

> Traffic light system -> live 12 Aug 2024; under 2 percent of job seekers at orange or red by Oct 2025

The system was legislated and operating with the promised sanctions and obligations, on the promised design.

- Promise (manifesto): [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/programmes/news-extras/story/2018902030/election-2023-rnz-s-guide-to-party-policy) - National 2023 welfare policy (traffic light system for Jobseeker Support), as summarised in RNZ's guide to party policy
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/green-light-welfare-works)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/government-begins-reset-welfare-system)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/welfare-sanctions-expand-job-searches-upskilling)

### Have 50,000 fewer people on the Jobseeker Support benefit, taking the number from a December 2023 baseline of 140,000 down to 90,000 by 2030.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Partly delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`welfare-national-jobseeker-target`

> 50,000 fewer on Jobseeker by 2030 -> 215,200 recorded at March 2026, target rated At Risk

The target date is 2030, so it remains live. But the measured number has moved away from the target, and DPMC itself rates it At Risk.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [dpmc.govt.nz](https://www.dpmc.govt.nz/our-programmes/government-targets) - Government Targets published by DPMC, set April 2024
- Evidence: [dpmc.govt.nz](https://www.dpmc.govt.nz/sites/default/files/2026-06/gt-factsheet-target-5-mar26.pdf)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/latest-figures-continue-year-year-increase-jobseekers-finding-work)

### Introduce FamilyBoost, a childcare tax rebate of up to $150 a fortnight (25 per cent of early childhood fees). It abates from $140,000 of household income and cuts out at $180,000, reaching about 130,000 households.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`welfare-national-familyboost`

> Up to $150 a fortnight for about 130,000 households -> about 60,000 families claiming by mid-2025, 249 on the full amount

The payment exists and was later made more generous. But it reached well under half the households promised, and very few received the advertised maximum.

- Promise (manifesto): [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/news/political/496895/national-s-tax-plan-and-costings-what-you-need-to-know) - National 2023 manifesto / 'Back Pocket Boost' tax plan, 30 August 2023
- Evidence: [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/news/politics/566156/familyboost-payment-increased-income-limit-raised)
- Evidence: [1news.co.nz](https://www.1news.co.nz/2025/05/14/nearly-a-quarter-of-familyboost-money-spent-on-admin/)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/greater-focus-work-will-reduce-child-poverty)

### Lift 17,000 more children out of material hardship by 2027 compared with 2023. Cut persistent child poverty from 9.4 per cent (98,900 children in the year to June 2023) to 8 per cent by 2035, lifting about 15,000 children out of persistent poverty.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`welfare-national-child-hardship-targets`

> 17,000 fewer children in material hardship by 2027 -> 156,600 children (13.4%) in material hardship in the year to June 2024, up from 143,700

The targets run to 2027 and 2035 and remain live. But the latest published data show material hardship higher than the 2023 baseline, rather than 17,000 children lower.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/government-sets-target-reduce-persistent-poverty) - Child and Youth Strategy targets announced by the Minister for Child Poverty Reduction, 13 December 2024
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/child-poverty-statistics-show-challenges-remain)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/greater-focus-work-will-reduce-child-poverty)

### Keep the superannuation age at 65.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`welfare-national-super-age-65`

> Superannuation age 65 -> unchanged as at August 2026

The commitment was to leave the age unchanged, and it has been left unchanged for the whole term to date.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/nzfirst/pages/4462/attachments/original/1700784896/National___NZF_Coalition_Agreement_signed_-_24_Nov_2023.pdf) - National-NZ First Coalition Agreement 2023
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/cost-living-support-over-14-million-kiwis)
- Evidence: [mcguinnessinstitute.org](https://www.mcguinnessinstitute.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/WP202007-202009-AnalysisOfThe2017Labour-FINAL.pdf)

### Save about $2 billion over the forecast period by returning the indexation of main benefits to inflation rather than average wage growth.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`gapfill-national-national-benefit-indexation-cpi`

> About $2b of forecast savings -> CPI indexation legislated Feb 2024, in force 1 April 2024 (4.66% adjustment)

The indexation change was legislated in the Government's first months and took effect on 1 April 2024, as costed in the fiscal plan.

- Promise (manifesto): [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/news/political/499024/election-2023-national-party-fiscal-plan-promises-lower-taxes-disciplined-government-spending) - National 2023 fiscal plan
- Evidence: [msd.govt.nz](https://www.msd.govt.nz/documents/about-msd-and-our-work/publications-resources/information-releases/cabinet-papers/2024/annual-general-adjustment-2024/paper-annual-general-adjustment-2024.pdf)
- Evidence: [parliament.nz](https://www.parliament.nz/en/pb/hansard-debates/rhr/combined/HansDeb_20240213_20240214_08)

## Treaty & Māori Policy

_Labour built new Treaty-based institutions and removed the poll veto on Māori wards, while the National-led coalition committed to unwinding much of that programme, disestablishing the Māori Health Authority, restoring ward referendums, repealing section 7AA, amending the Marine and Coastal Area Act and reviewing Treaty principles clauses in statute, with several of those commitments still incomplete as at August 2026._

### Make te reo Māori a core curriculum subject in all state primary and secondary schools by 2025, alongside additional resourcing for kaupapa Māori education.

**Greens** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Not delivered**

### Remove the provisions of the Local Electoral Act that let five per cent of electors force a binding poll. Those polls could overturn a council decision to establish Māori wards or constituencies.

### Stop all work on He Puapua. Confirm that the Government does not recognise the United Nations Declaration on the Rights of Indigenous Peoples as having any binding legal effect on New Zealand.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Partly delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`treaty-nzfirst-he-puapua-undrip`

There is no evidence of continuing work on He Puapua or a Declaration Plan after the change of government. But no published document confirming the work programme was formally closed was located.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/nzfirst/pages/4462/attachments/original/1700784896/National___NZF_Coalition_Agreement_signed_-_24_Nov_2023.pdf) - National-NZ First Coalition Agreement, signed 24 November 2023
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/He_Puapua)
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sixth_National_Government_of_New_Zealand)

### Legislate to make English an official language of New Zealand.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Not delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`treaty-nzfirst-english-official-language`

> Legislate English as an official language -> 0 Acts passed; official languages remain te reo Māori (1987) and NZ Sign Language (2006)

English still had no statutory official language status with under three months of the term remaining.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/nzfirst/pages/4462/attachments/original/1700784896/National___NZF_Coalition_Agreement_signed_-_24_Nov_2023.pdf) - National-NZ First Coalition Agreement, signed 24 November 2023
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Languages_of_New_Zealand)

### Restore the right to a local referendum on setting up or continuing to use Māori wards. This includes requiring a referendum at the next local body elections on any wards established without one.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`treaty-national-maori-wards-referendums`

> Binding referendums restored -> Act passed 30 July 2024, 42 polls held in 2025, 24 councils voted to remove wards and 18 to retain

Legislation restored the referendum right within nine months, and the binding polls were held at the 2025 local elections exactly as promised.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [russellmcveagh.com](https://www.russellmcveagh.com/media/haxjinrp/national-act-nz-first-coalition-government-policies-constitution-and-rights.pdf) - National-ACT and National-NZ First Coalition Agreements 2023
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C4%81ori_wards_and_constituencies)
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sixth_National_Government_of_New_Zealand)

### Repeal section 7AA of the Oranga Tamariki Act 1989.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`treaty-national-oranga-tamariki-7aa`

> Repeal s7AA -> Oranga Tamariki (Repeal of Section 7AA) Amendment Act, royal assent 3 April 2025

Section 7AA was repealed by Act of Parliament inside the term, as promised.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [russellmcveagh.com](https://www.russellmcveagh.com/media/haxjinrp/national-act-nz-first-coalition-government-policies-constitution-and-rights.pdf) - 2023 coalition commitments on constitution and rights, compiled from the National-ACT and National-NZ First agreements
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oranga_Tamariki_Act_1989)

### Remove co-governance from the delivery of public services.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Largely delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`treaty-national-cogovernance-public-services`

> Co-governance removed from public service delivery -> Māori Health Authority, Three Waters representative groups and s7AA all repealed between Feb 2024 and Apr 2025

The named co-governance arrangements in health, water and care of children were all removed. Co-governance arrangements in conservation, Treaty settlement bodies and some council structures continued.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [russellmcveagh.com](https://www.russellmcveagh.com/media/haxjinrp/national-act-nz-first-coalition-government-policies-constitution-and-rights.pdf) - National-ACT and National-NZ First Coalition Agreements 2023 (the commitment appears in both)
- Evidence: [buddlefindlay.com](https://www.buddlefindlay.com/insights/legislation-passed-to-disestablish-the-maori-health-authority-te-aka-whai-ora/)
- Evidence: [waitangitribunal.govt.nz](https://www.waitangitribunal.govt.nz/en/news/tribunal-releases-report-on-disestablishment-of-te-aka-whai-ora)
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_Waters_reform_programme)
