## Immigration

_Both governments promised to make the immigration system more selective, but net migration swung from a promised cut through a closed border to a record inflow and back again, and immigration is where the gap between New Zealand First's manifestos and the coalition agreements it signed is widest._

### Increase New Zealand's annual refugee quota to around 6,000 places.

**Greens** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Not delivered**  
`immigration-labour-greens-refugee-quota-6000`

> quota of about 6,000 sought -> quota reached 1,500 from July 2020

The quota reached 1,500, a quarter of the level the Greens sought, and no further increase followed.

- Promise (manifesto): [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/news/political/366801/refugee-quota-to-rise-from-1000-to-1500) - Green Party 2017 refugee policy
- Evidence: [immigration.govt.nz](https://www.immigration.govt.nz/about-us/our-strategies-and-programmes/supporting-refugees-and-asylum-seekers/refugee-resettlement-strategy/increasing-new-zealands-refugee-quota/)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/refugee-quota-increases-1500-2020)

### Reduce net migration by 20,000 to 30,000 a year, mainly by tightening student visas and low-skilled work visas.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Not delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`immigration-labour-net-migration-cut`

> cut of 20,000-30,000 a year promised -> net migration gain of 126,000 in the December 2023 year, a record

By the October 2023 handover, net migration was at a record high rather than 20,000-30,000 lower. The policy package was never scaled to deliver the promised cut.

- Promise (manifesto): [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/news/political/342077/ardern-confirms-labour-s-immigration-target-to-hold-sway) - Labour 2017 immigration policy
- Evidence: [newsroom.co.nz](https://newsroom.co.nz/2018/06/01/immigration-cuts-finally-revealed/)
- Evidence: [stats.govt.nz](https://www.stats.govt.nz/news/net-migration-remains-near-record-level/)
- Evidence: [stats.govt.nz](https://www.stats.govt.nz/information-releases/international-migration-october-2023/)

### Raise the annual refugee quota to 1,500 within three years, with funding for resettlement services.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered**  
`immigration-labour-refugee-quota-1500`

> quota of 1,500 promised -> quota lifted from 1,000 to 1,500 from 1 July 2020; 797 arrivals in the year to June 2020

The quota was lifted to 1,500 on the promised timetable. Border closures then meant actual arrivals fell far short of the quota for several years.

- Promise (manifesto): [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/news/political/366801/refugee-quota-to-rise-from-1000-to-1500) - Labour 2017 immigration policy
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/refugee-quota-increases-1500-2020)
- Evidence: [immigration.govt.nz](https://www.immigration.govt.nz/about-us/our-strategies-and-programmes/supporting-refugees-and-asylum-seekers/refugee-resettlement-strategy/increasing-new-zealands-refugee-quota/)
- Evidence: [waikato.ac.nz](https://www.waikato.ac.nz/assets/Uploads/News-and-events/News/News/2021/new-zealand-refugee-quota-august-2020.pdf)

### Provide a one-off simplified residence pathway for about 165,000 migrants already in New Zealand on eligible work visas, and decide most applications within a year.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered**  
`immigration-labour-2021-resident-visa`

> about 165,000 migrants promised a pathway -> 103,961 applications approved covering 212,649 residents

The pathway was opened on the announced timetable and granted residence to more people than the announcement estimated.

- Promise (manifesto): [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/one-residence-pathway-provides-certainty-migrants-and-business) - Labour government policy announcement, 30 September 2021 (one-off 2021 Resident Visa)
- Evidence: [immigration.govt.nz](https://www.immigration.govt.nz/about-us/news-centre/2021-resident-visa/)

### Cut net migration to around 10,000 a year.

**NZ First** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Superseded**  
`immigration-labour-nzf-10000-net-migration`

> 10,000 net migration a year sought -> no migration number in the signed coalition agreement

The 10,000 target was dropped in the coalition negotiation and does not appear in the signed agreement.

- Promise (manifesto): [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/news/political/342077/ardern-confirms-labour-s-immigration-target-to-hold-sway) - NZ First 2017 immigration policy
- Evidence: [mcguinnessinstitute.org](https://www.mcguinnessinstitute.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/WP202007-202009-AnalysisOfThe2017Labour-FINAL.pdf)

### Ensure work visas issued reflect genuine skills shortages and cut down on low quality international education courses.

**NZ First** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Largely delivered**  
`immigration-labour-nzf-work-student-visas`

The visa settings were changed broadly as described. The effect on overall student numbers was smaller and less even than intended.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [mcguinnessinstitute.org](https://www.mcguinnessinstitute.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/WP202007-202009-AnalysisOfThe2017Labour-FINAL.pdf) - Labour-NZ First coalition agreement, October 2017
- Evidence: [newsroom.co.nz](https://newsroom.co.nz/2018/06/01/immigration-cuts-finally-revealed/)
- Evidence: [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/news/national/391498/the-unintended-consequences-of-tightening-student-visas)

### Take serious action on migrant exploitation, particularly of international students.

**NZ First** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Partly delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`immigration-labour-nzf-migrant-exploitation`

> action on migrant exploitation promised -> Migrant Exploitation Protection Visa from 1 Jul 2021, but 145 employer accreditations revoked and a critical review of the AEWV in Feb 2024

Real protections were built. But the same government's flagship work visa scheme created large-scale exploitation risk, and a formal review found the agency should have done more to prevent it.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [mcguinnessinstitute.org](https://www.mcguinnessinstitute.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/WP202007-202009-AnalysisOfThe2017Labour-FINAL.pdf) - Labour-NZ First coalition agreement, October 2017
- Evidence: [mbie.govt.nz](https://www.mbie.govt.nz/immigration-and-tourism/immigration/temporary-migrant-worker-exploitation-review/)
- Evidence: [mbie.govt.nz](https://www.mbie.govt.nz/about/news/mbie-responds-to-review-on-accredited-employer-work-visa)
- Evidence: [publicservice.govt.nz](https://www.publicservice.govt.nz/assets/Assurance-review-of-the-operation-of-the-AEWV-scheme-Final-Report.pdf)

### Remove the median wage requirement from skilled migrant and employer-assisted work visas. Allow family members of visa holders to work, and increase the Recognised Seasonal Employer worker cap.

**ACT** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Largely delivered**  
`immigration-national-act-median-wage-removal`

> median wage requirement removed -> replaced with market-rate pay requirement for AEWV roles

The median wage change and the skilled migrant rework were delivered. The wider package was implemented in stages, with some thresholds only taking effect in 2026.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [vialtopartners.com](https://vialtopartners.com/regional-alerts/new-zealand-immigration-potential-immigration-policy-changes) - National-ACT coalition agreement, 24 November 2023
- Evidence: [ojs.aut.ac.nz](https://ojs.aut.ac.nz/nzjer/article/download/187/105/)
- Evidence: [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/news/political/531213/stanford-looks-to-trusted-employers-to-help-unblock-work-visas-logjam)

### Maintain the parent category visa cap at 1,000 places, with sponsors required to adequately support family members.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Superseded**  
`immigration-national-nzf-parent-cap-1000`

> cap of 1,000 sought -> 2,500 parent resident approvals in principle issued in 2025/26

The 1,000 cap was not carried into either coalition agreement and the parent categories operated well above it.

- Promise (manifesto): [nzfirst.nz](https://www.nzfirst.nz/2023_policies) - NZ First 2023 election policies. Immigration
- Evidence: [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/nzfirst/pages/4462/attachments/original/1700784896/National___NZF_Coalition_Agreement_signed_-_24_Nov_2023.pdf)
- Evidence: [immigration.govt.nz](https://www.immigration.govt.nz/about-us/news-centre/overview-of-parent-visas-and-application-numbers)

### Replace the Accredited Employer Work Visa with a Critical Skill and Labour Shortage Visa that prioritises New Zealand workers.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Superseded**  
`immigration-national-nzf-replace-aewv`

> AEWV replacement sought -> AEWV retained and amended

The replacement visa was dropped in the coalition negotiation. In its place came a commitment to improve the existing scheme, which is what happened.

- Promise (manifesto): [nzfirst.nz](https://www.nzfirst.nz/2023_policies) - NZ First 2023 election policies. Immigration
- Evidence: [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/nzfirst/pages/4462/attachments/original/1700784896/National___NZF_Coalition_Agreement_signed_-_24_Nov_2023.pdf)
- Evidence: [ojs.aut.ac.nz](https://ojs.aut.ac.nz/nzjer/article/download/187/105/)

### Improve the Accredited Employer Work Visa so the immigration system attracts the workers and skills New Zealand needs. Ensure Immigration New Zealand undertakes proper risk management and verification, and ensure those responsible for abusing migrant workers face consequences.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Largely delivered**  
`immigration-national-nzf-improve-aewv`

> AEWV reform promised -> 145 accreditations revoked, review recommendations accepted Feb 2024; net migration 130,900 (Feb 2024 year) to 17,600 (Jun 2026 year)

The scheme was reviewed, tightened and then rebuilt broadly as the agreement described. The 'Essential Worker' workforce planning mechanism in the same agreement remains an investigation rather than a system.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/nzfirst/pages/4462/attachments/original/1700784896/National___NZF_Coalition_Agreement_signed_-_24_Nov_2023.pdf) - National-NZ First coalition agreement, 24 November 2023
- Evidence: [mbie.govt.nz](https://www.mbie.govt.nz/about/news/mbie-responds-to-review-on-accredited-employer-work-visa)
- Evidence: [ojs.aut.ac.nz](https://ojs.aut.ac.nz/nzjer/article/download/187/105/)
- Evidence: [scoop.co.nz](https://www.scoop.co.nz/stories/AK2608/S00432/net-migration-gain-of-17600.htm)

### Introduce a multiple-entry Parent Visa Boost valid for five years and renewable for a further five. Children or grandchildren sponsor it, health insurance is compulsory, and there is no access to NZ Super or other entitlements.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`immigration-national-parent-boost-visa`

> 5-year renewable parent visitor visa promised -> opened 29 Sep 2025; 708 applications and 230 approvals by 2 Feb 2026

The visa was created with the duration, renewal, sponsorship and insurance conditions promised, and is open.

- Promise (manifesto): [ericastanfordmp.co.nz](https://www.ericastanfordmp.co.nz/post/national-to-introduce-parent-visa-boost) - National 2023 immigration policy announcement, 23 September 2023
- Evidence: [immigration.govt.nz](https://www.immigration.govt.nz/about-us/news-centre/overview-of-parent-visas-and-application-numbers)
- Evidence: [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/news/political/531213/stanford-looks-to-trusted-employers-to-help-unblock-work-visas-logjam)

### Replace the investor visa category with settings that actually attract international investment to New Zealand.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`immigration-national-investor-visa`

> 116 applications over 2.5 years under old settings -> 837 applications and NZ$2.3b transferred since April 2025

The category was rebuilt, and application and investment volumes rose by an order of magnitude against the previous settings.

- Promise (manifesto): [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/news/political/531213/stanford-looks-to-trusted-employers-to-help-unblock-work-visas-logjam) - National 2023 policy to attract top talent and investment; confirmed as an investor visa replacement by Immigration Minister Erica Stanford in 2024
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/flood-interest-invest-new-zealand)
- Evidence: [immigration.govt.nz](https://www.immigration.govt.nz/about-us/news-centre/investor-category)

### Revive the international education sector after the COVID-19 collapse.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **In progress**  
`immigration-national-international-education`

> 83,400 enrolments in 2024 -> 85,535 in Jan-Aug 2025; targets of 105,000 by 2027 and NZ$7.2b exports by 2034

Enrolments and export earnings are rising and the visa settings are in place. The enrolment and export targets run to 2027 and 2034, beyond this term.

- Promise (manifesto): [ericastanfordmp.co.nz](https://www.ericastanfordmp.co.nz/press) - National 2023 international education policy announcement, 21 September 2023
- Evidence: [monitor.icef.com](https://monitor.icef.com/2026/03/new-zealand-expands-post-study-work-opportunities-for-international-students/)
- Evidence: [immigration.govt.nz](https://www.immigration.govt.nz/about-us/news-centre/new-and-updated-post-study-work-visa-options/)

## Resource Management

_Both governments promised to replace the Resource Management Act 1991: Labour passed two replacement statutes in August 2023 that lasted four months, and the National-led coalition repealed them, amended the RMA twice, built a permanent fast-track approvals regime and introduced its own two-bill replacement, which was still before Parliament as at August 2026._

### Repeal the Resource Management Act 1991 and replace it with three new laws (a Natural and Built Environments Act, a Spatial Planning Act and a Climate Change Adaptation Act), acting on the recommendations of the Randerson review.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Largely delivered**  
`rma-labour-repeal-replace-rma`

> 3 replacement acts promised -> 2 passed (Aug 2023), 0 in operation at handover

Two of the three promised statutes were passed inside the term. The third was never introduced, and neither Act had begun operating before the handover.

- Promise (manifesto): [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Resource_Management_Act_1991) - Labour 2020 campaign commitment to repeal and replace the RMA, given effect by the 10 February 2021 Government announcement following the Randerson review
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_and_Built_Environment_Act_2023)
- Evidence: [legislation.govt.nz](https://www.legislation.govt.nz/act/public/2023/0046/latest/LMS501892.html)
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Resource_Management_Act_1991)

### Pass a Climate Change Adaptation Act as the third pillar of the resource management reform, to deal with managed retreat and the funding of climate adaptation.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Not delivered**  
`rma-labour-climate-adaptation-act`

> Climate Change Adaptation Act promised -> bill never introduced

The promised third Act was never introduced, let alone passed, in either term.

- Promise (manifesto): [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Resource_Management_Act_1991) - Labour-led Government's February 2021 three-law RMA replacement programme (Climate Change Adaptation Act)
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Resource_Management_Act_1991)
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_and_Built_Environment_Act_2023)

### Require every region to prepare a long-term regional spatial strategy, agreed between councils, central government and iwi, to co-ordinate land use, infrastructure and development.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered**  
`rma-labour-spatial-planning`

> Regional spatial strategies legislated Aug 2023 -> 0 completed before repeal Dec 2023

The statute promised was enacted within the term, judged as at the October 2023 handover. Its later repeal was the successor government's decision.

- Promise (manifesto): [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spatial_Planning_Act_2023) - Labour-led Government's RMA replacement programme. Spatial Planning Act
- Evidence: [legislation.govt.nz](https://www.legislation.govt.nz/act/public/2023/0047/latest/LMS545761.html)
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spatial_Planning_Act_2023)

### Use a National Policy Statement on Urban Development to force councils to allow greater building height and density near city centres and rapid transit. It would also make councils remove minimum car parking requirements from district plans.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered**  
`rma-labour-nps-urban-development`

> 6-storey enablement near centres and rapid transit -> in force Aug 2020, parking minimums gone by Feb 2022

The national policy statement was issued, took effect and was implemented through council plan changes within the term. It survived the change of government.

- Promise (manifesto): [thespinoff.co.nz](https://thespinoff.co.nz/politics/12-10-2023/election-2023-the-housing-policies-in-two-minutes) - Labour's urban growth agenda. National Policy Statement on Urban Development 2020
- Evidence: [hud.govt.nz](https://www.hud.govt.nz/our-work/going-for-housing-growth-programme)
- Evidence: [thespinoff.co.nz](https://thespinoff.co.nz/politics/12-10-2023/election-2023-the-housing-policies-in-two-minutes)

### Legislate Medium Density Residential Standards so that up to three homes of up to three storeys can be built on most residential sites in the largest cities without a resource consent.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered**  
`rma-labour-mdrs-bipartisan`

> 3 homes of 3 storeys on most sites -> legislated Dec 2021, applied in tier 1 plans from Aug 2022

The density standards were legislated and applied through council plans within the term. Judged at the October 2023 handover the promise was met, and the partial unwinding came afterwards.

- Promise (manifesto): [thespinoff.co.nz](https://thespinoff.co.nz/politics/12-10-2023/election-2023-the-housing-policies-in-two-minutes) - Labour-National bipartisan housing density agreement, October 2021, enacted as the Resource Management (Enabling Housing Supply and Other Matters) Amendment Act 2021
- Evidence: [hud.govt.nz](https://www.hud.govt.nz/our-work/going-for-housing-growth-programme)
- Evidence: [thespinoff.co.nz](https://thespinoff.co.nz/politics/12-10-2023/election-2023-the-housing-policies-in-two-minutes)

### Replace the Resource Management Act 1991 with new resource management laws premised on the enjoyment of property rights as a guiding principle.

**ACT** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`rma-act-property-rights-replacement`

> RMA replacement premised on property rights -> bills before select committee Aug 2026, system due 2029

The property-rights principle has been carried into the drafted replacement bills. But the laws themselves were not passed as at August 2026, and the term is unfinished.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/actnz/mailings/6945/attachments/original/National_ACT_Agreement.pdf) - National-ACT Coalition Agreement 2023, replace the RMA with new resource management laws premised on the enjoyment of property rights as a guiding principle
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Resource_Management_Act_1991)
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Bishop)

### Legislate to make the Medium Density Residential Standards optional for councils, requiring councils to ratify any use of the MDRS including in existing zones.

**ACT** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`rma-act-mdrs-ratification`

> MDRS ratification for all councils -> delivered for 2 of the affected councils by Aug 2026

The commitment was legislated for two councils and announced as policy for the rest. The general ratification requirement in the agreement had not been delivered nearly three years in.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/actnz/mailings/6945/attachments/original/National_ACT_Agreement.pdf) - National-ACT Coalition Agreement 2023, legislate to make the MDRS optional for councils, with councils to ratify any use of MDRS including existing zones
- Evidence: [hud.govt.nz](https://www.hud.govt.nz/our-work/going-for-housing-growth-programme)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/going-housing-growth-stage-one-unveiled)

### Repeal Labour's planning laws and temporarily reinstate the Resource Management Act. Then replace it with legislation modelled on Ireland's Planning and Development Act and England's Town and Country Planning Act, restricting third party appeals and replacing the Environment Court.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`rma-nzfirst-planning-law-model`

> Repeal then replace on the Irish/English model -> repeal done Dec 2023, replacement bills unpassed Aug 2026

The repeal and temporary reinstatement happened as promised. But the specific replacement model, the appeal restrictions and the replacement of the Environment Court had not been enacted as at August 2026.

- Promise (manifesto): [nzfirst.nz](https://www.nzfirst.nz/2023_policies) - New Zealand First 2023 Policy Manifesto, planning legislation
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Resource_Management_Act_1991)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/nba-and-spa-successfully-repealed)

### Stop the co-governance model built into Labour's planning legislation, including the joint council and iwi planning committees created under the new system.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Largely delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`rma-nzfirst-planning-cogovernance`

The specific committee structures the party objected to were abolished with the repeal, though iwi involvement in the planning system continues in other forms.

- Promise (manifesto): [nzfirst.nz](https://www.nzfirst.nz/2023_policies) - New Zealand First 2023 Policy Manifesto, opposition to 50:50 council/iwi planning committees
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Resource_Management_Act_1991)
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fast-track_Approvals_Act_2024)

### Repeal the Natural and Built Environment Act 2023 and the Spatial Planning Act 2023 by Christmas 2023.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`rma-national-repeal-nba-spa`

> Repeal by Christmas 2023 promised -> passed 19 Dec 2023, assent 23 Dec 2023

Both Acts were repealed before Christmas 2023, exactly as the coalition agreement and 100-day plan specified.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/actnz/mailings/6945/attachments/original/National_ACT_Agreement.pdf) - National-ACT Coalition Agreement 2023 and National's 100-day plan, repeal the Natural and Built Environment Act 2023 and the Spatial Planning Act 2023 by Christmas
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/nba-and-spa-successfully-repealed)
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Resource_Management_Act_1991)
- Evidence: [thespinoff.co.nz](https://thespinoff.co.nz/politics/28-11-2023/the-ctrl-z-coalition-all-the-repeals-and-reversals-planned-by-the-new-government)

### Replace the Resource Management Act 1991 with two new statutes, one for the natural environment and one for planning and development, during this parliamentary term.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`rma-national-two-act-replacement`

> 100+ plans -> 17 regional combined plans proposed; bills at select committee Aug 2026

The replacement bills were introduced and passed first reading, but they had not been enacted as at August 2026. The new system is not scheduled to operate until 2029, so the term is not finished.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/actnz/mailings/6945/attachments/original/National_ACT_Agreement.pdf) - National-ACT Coalition Agreement 2023, replace the Resource Management Act 1991 with new resource management laws
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Resource_Management_Act_1991)
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Bishop)

### Establish a permanent fast-track one-stop-shop consenting and permitting regime for regional and national projects of significance.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`rma-national-fast-track-onestop`

> One-stop-shop promised -> Act in force 23 Dec 2024 with 149 listed projects

A permanent one-stop-shop regime was legislated within the first term and is issuing decisions, although the final Act differs from the introduced Bill in who makes the decision.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/nzfirst/pages/4462/attachments/original/1700784896/National___NZF_Coalition_Agreement_signed_-_24_Nov_2023.pdf) - National-NZ First Coalition Agreement 2023, establish a fast-track one-stop-shop consenting and permitting process for regional and national projects of significance
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fast-track_Approvals_Act_2024)
- Evidence: [fasttrack.govt.nz](https://www.fasttrack.govt.nz/projects/southland-wind-farm)
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_in_New_Zealand)

### Amend the Resource Management Act 1991 to make it easier to consent new infrastructure including renewable energy, to allow farmers to farm, and to enable aquaculture and other primary industries.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`rma-national-phase-one-amendments`

> Phase one amendments -> 2 Acts passed Aug and Oct 2024

The targeted amendments named in the agreement were passed inside the first year of the term.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/nzfirst/pages/4462/attachments/original/1700784896/National___NZF_Coalition_Agreement_signed_-_24_Nov_2023.pdf) - National-NZ First Coalition Agreement 2023, amend the RMA to make it easier to consent new infrastructure, allow farmers to farm and enable primary industries
- Evidence: [dlapiper.com](https://www.dlapiper.com/en/insights/publications/2024/11/legislation-changes-in-resource-management-in-new-zealand)
- Evidence: [simpsongrierson.com](https://www.simpsongrierson.com/insights-news/legal-updates/rma-amendments-signal-major-change-of-direction-for-freshwater-planning-and-consenting)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/significant-natural-areas-requirement-be-suspended)

### Change the consenting system so more houses get built. That includes requiring councils to live-zone at least 30 years of feasible housing capacity instead of three, and removing planning rules such as minimum floor areas, balcony requirements and Auckland's Rural-Urban Boundary.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Largely delivered**  
`rma-national-phase-two-consenting`

> 3 years of live-zoned housing capacity -> 30 years required, in force Aug 2025

The main consenting and zoning changes are law and in force. But parts of the package for councils outside Auckland and Christchurch were still in consultation as at August 2026.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/nzfirst/pages/4462/attachments/original/1700784896/National___NZF_Coalition_Agreement_signed_-_24_Nov_2023.pdf) - National-NZ First Coalition Agreement 2023, amend the RMA to get more houses built; Going for Housing Growth stage one, 4 July 2024
- Evidence: [hud.govt.nz](https://www.hud.govt.nz/our-work/going-for-housing-growth-programme)
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Bishop)

### Rewrite the national direction that sits under the resource management system, starting with replacing the National Policy Statement for Freshwater Management 2020 and the freshwater national environmental standards.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`rma-national-national-direction-rewrite`

> NPS-FM 2020 replacement promised -> amended NPS-FM gazetted Dec 2025, full package incomplete

Some instruments have been amended or replaced, including the freshwater national policy statement in December 2025. But the promised full rewrite of national direction is unfinished, and partly depends on legislation still before Parliament.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/nzfirst/pages/4462/attachments/original/1700784896/National___NZF_Coalition_Agreement_signed_-_24_Nov_2023.pdf) - National-NZ First Coalition Agreement 2023, replace the National Policy Statement for Freshwater Management 2020 and the National Environmental Standards for Freshwater, and review national direction
- Evidence: [environment.govt.nz](https://environment.govt.nz/assets/publications/npsfm-2020-amended-december-2025.pdf)
- Evidence: [simpsongrierson.com](https://www.simpsongrierson.com/insights-news/legal-updates/rma-amendments-signal-major-change-of-direction-for-freshwater-planning-and-consenting)
- Evidence: [russellmcveagh.com](https://www.russellmcveagh.com/insights-news/fresh-updates-in-the-freshwater-space/)

### Give councils the ability to withdraw from the mandatory Medium Density Residential Standards, while keeping the National Policy Statement on Urban Development and requiring councils to zone for growth.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`rma-national-mdrs-optional`

> MDRS optional for all councils promised -> opt-out legislated for Auckland and Christchurch only by Aug 2026

Optionality was legislated for two cities and promised for the rest. Most councils still had the standards imposed on them nearly three years into the term.

- Promise (manifesto): [thespinoff.co.nz](https://thespinoff.co.nz/politics/12-10-2023/election-2023-the-housing-policies-in-two-minutes) - National 2023 housing and planning policy, give councils the ability to withdraw from the MDRS while keeping the NPS-UD
- Evidence: [hud.govt.nz](https://www.hud.govt.nz/our-work/going-for-housing-growth-programme)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/going-housing-growth-stage-one-unveiled)


---

## Primary Industries

_The 2017-2023 government layered new freshwater, emissions and animal welfare rules onto farming while trying to design a farm-level emissions price, and the 2023 coalition campaigned on unwinding much of that regulation, protecting farmland from carbon forestry and doubling export earnings._

### Provide assistance to the agricultural sector to reduce biological emissions and improve water quality, and to shift to more diverse and sustainable land use including more forestry.

**Greens** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`primary-greens-agriculture-emissions-assistance`

> Farm-level emissions pricing designed under He Waka Eke Noa -> no pricing system in force by Oct 2023; scheme disbanded in 2024

Research, planting and partnership assistance were delivered, but the pricing and land-use transition the commitment pointed to never took effect.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [interest.co.nz](https://www.interest.co.nz/sites/default/files/embedded_images/Labour%20and%20Green%20Party%20Confidence%20and%20Supply%20Agreement.pdf) - Labour-Green Confidence and Supply Agreement 2017
- Evidence: [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/news/country/476451/he-waka-eke-noa-government-proposes-farm-level-system-by-2025)
- Evidence: [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/news/political/519248/coalition-exempts-farmers-from-ets-sets-up-fresh-working-group)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/agriculture-come-out-ets)

### Improve water quality and prioritise achieving healthy rivers, lakes and aquifers, with stronger regulatory instruments, funding for freshwater enhancement and a wind-down of Government support for irrigation.

**Greens** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Partly delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`primary-greens-wind-down-irrigation-support`

> Wind down support for irrigation -> existing Crown Irrigation commitments honoured in full

Stronger freshwater regulation and enhancement funding arrived. The irrigation wind-down was constrained by a competing commitment in the other coalition agreement.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [interest.co.nz](https://www.interest.co.nz/sites/default/files/embedded_images/Labour%20and%20Green%20Party%20Confidence%20and%20Supply%20Agreement.pdf) - Labour-Green Confidence and Supply Agreement 2017
- Evidence: [mcguinnessinstitute.org](https://www.mcguinnessinstitute.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/WP202007-202009-AnalysisOfThe2017Labour-FINAL.pdf)
- Evidence: [environment.govt.nz](https://environment.govt.nz/assets/publications/npsfm-2020-amended-december-2025.pdf)
- Evidence: [russellmcveagh.com](https://www.russellmcveagh.com/insights-news/fresh-updates-in-the-freshwater-space/)

### Set higher water quality standards for rural as well as urban waterways, using measurements that take account of seasonal differences.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`primary-labour-on-farm-freshwater-rules`

> Higher rural water standards -> NPS-FM 2020, NES-Freshwater and stock exclusion rules in force 3 Sept 2020; farm plans started in 5 regions by 2023

The rules were made and came into force. The on-farm instruments were still being phased in region by region when the term ended.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [mcguinnessinstitute.org](https://www.mcguinnessinstitute.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/WP202007-202009-AnalysisOfThe2017Labour-FINAL.pdf) - Labour-NZ First Coalition Agreement 2017, priority 47, higher water quality standards for urban and rural
- Evidence: [environment.govt.nz](https://environment.govt.nz/assets/publications/npsfm-2020-amended-december-2025.pdf)
- Evidence: [russellmcveagh.com](https://www.russellmcveagh.com/insights-news/fresh-updates-in-the-freshwater-space/)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/freshwater-farm-plan-systems-be-improved)

### Increase the resourcing of Biosecurity New Zealand within the Ministry for Primary Industries.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered**  
`primary-labour-biosecurity-funding`

Recorded as fully implemented in independent tracking of the coalition agreement.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [mcguinnessinstitute.org](https://www.mcguinnessinstitute.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/WP202007-202009-AnalysisOfThe2017Labour-FINAL.pdf) - Labour-NZ First Coalition Agreement 2017, priority 4, increase MPI Biosecurity New Zealand resourcing
- Evidence: [mcguinnessinstitute.org](https://www.mcguinnessinstitute.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/WP202007-202009-AnalysisOfThe2017Labour-FINAL.pdf)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/big-step-forward-mbovis-programme)

### Hold a Select Committee inquiry into biosecurity.

**NZ First** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Not delivered**  
`primary-nzf-biosecurity-inquiry`

Recorded as not implemented. The inquiry never took place.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [mcguinnessinstitute.org](https://www.mcguinnessinstitute.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/WP202007-202009-AnalysisOfThe2017Labour-FINAL.pdf) - Labour-NZ First Coalition Agreement 2017, priority 4a. Select Committee inquiry into biosecurity
- Evidence: [mcguinnessinstitute.org](https://www.mcguinnessinstitute.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/WP202007-202009-AnalysisOfThe2017Labour-FINAL.pdf)

### Re-establish a New Zealand Forestry Service, located in regional New Zealand.

**NZ First** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered**  
`primary-nzf-forestry-service`

> Re-establish a Forestry Service -> Te Uru Rākau established, based in Rotorua

The agency was created and located in a regional centre as promised.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [mcguinnessinstitute.org](https://www.mcguinnessinstitute.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/WP202007-202009-AnalysisOfThe2017Labour-FINAL.pdf) - Labour-NZ First Coalition Agreement 2017, priority 3, re-establish the New Zealand Forestry Service
- Evidence: [mcguinnessinstitute.org](https://www.mcguinnessinstitute.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/WP202007-202009-AnalysisOfThe2017Labour-FINAL.pdf)

### Recognise and promote the potential of aquaculture as a driver of regional economic growth.

**NZ First** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered**  
`primary-nzf-aquaculture-growth`

> Aquaculture strategy -> published, targeting a $3 billion industry by 2035

The strategy the commitment called for was published within the term. The $3 billion figure is a 2035 goal, not a term commitment.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [mcguinnessinstitute.org](https://www.mcguinnessinstitute.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/WP202007-202009-AnalysisOfThe2017Labour-FINAL.pdf) - Labour-NZ First Coalition Agreement 2017, priority 6, recognise the potential for aquaculture

### Examine agricultural debt mediation, along with receivership fees and charges.

**NZ First** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered**  
`primary-nzf-farm-debt-mediation`

> Examine farm debt mediation -> Farm Debt Mediation Act passed, Royal assent December 2019

The commitment was to examine the issue and it produced legislation within the term.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [mcguinnessinstitute.org](https://www.mcguinnessinstitute.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/WP202007-202009-AnalysisOfThe2017Labour-FINAL.pdf) - Labour-NZ First Coalition Agreement 2017, priority 7, examination of agricultural debt mediation
- Evidence: [mcguinnessinstitute.org](https://www.mcguinnessinstitute.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/WP202007-202009-AnalysisOfThe2017Labour-FINAL.pdf)

### Honour the Crown's existing irrigation investment commitments.

**NZ First** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered**  
`primary-nzf-crown-irrigation`

Existing commitments were honoured, which is what the agreement required.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [mcguinnessinstitute.org](https://www.mcguinnessinstitute.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/WP202007-202009-AnalysisOfThe2017Labour-FINAL.pdf) - Labour-NZ First Coalition Agreement 2017, priority 5, honour existing Crown Irrigation investment commitments
- Evidence: [mcguinnessinstitute.org](https://www.mcguinnessinstitute.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/WP202007-202009-AnalysisOfThe2017Labour-FINAL.pdf)
- Evidence: [interest.co.nz](https://www.interest.co.nz/sites/default/files/embedded_images/Labour%20and%20Green%20Party%20Confidence%20and%20Supply%20Agreement.pdf)

### Cease the implementation of new Significant Natural Areas and seek advice on the operation of existing Significant Natural Areas.

**ACT** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`primary-act-significant-natural-areas`

> Cease new SNAs -> three-year suspension announced 14 March 2024, legislated 24 October 2024

Both limbs of the commitment (stopping new SNAs and reviewing existing ones) were actioned and legislated within the first year.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/actnz/mailings/6945/attachments/original/National_ACT_Agreement.pdf) - National-ACT Coalition Agreement 2023
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/significant-natural-areas-requirement-be-suspended)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/suspension-new-snas-passes-its-third-reading)
- Evidence: [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/news/politics/511689/government-drops-need-for-councils-to-comply-with-significant-natural-areas-provisions)

### Stop exotic planting on Land Use Capability class 1, 2, 3, 4 and 5 land from entering the Emissions Trading Scheme for carbon sequestration. The aim is to protect productive farmland from carbon farming.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`primary-nzf-luc-forestry-ets-ban`

> No exotic ETS forestry on LUC 1-5 -> legislated 2025, with LUC 6 capped at 15,000 hectares a year

The LUC 1-5 restriction the manifesto specified was legislated, with an added annual cap on LUC 6 and a 25 per cent on-farm allowance.

- Promise (manifesto): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/nzfirst/pages/4158/attachments/original/1696631089/2023-New-Zealand-First-Policy-Manifesto-1.pdf) - New Zealand First 2023 Policy Manifesto: 'Right tree, right place'
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/legislation-introduced-restrict-farm-forest-conversions)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/protecting-nz-food-production-and-ets-credibility)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/farm-forest-ban-passes-first-reading)

### Direct government agencies, where practical and appropriate, to prefer woollen fibres rather than artificial fibres in government buildings.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`primary-nzf-wool-in-government-buildings`

> Prefer wool in government buildings -> Kāinga Ora carpet tender reopened to wool Jan 2025; detailed direction still being worked through

The one clear procurement outcome was a single agency reopening a tender. Sector investment has been large, but a whole-of-government wool preference direction has not been published.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/nzfirst/pages/4462/attachments/original/1700784896/National___NZF_Coalition_Agreement_signed_-_24_Nov_2023.pdf) - National-NZ First Coalition Agreement 2023
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/ministers-welcome-k%C4%81inga-ora%E2%80%99s-revised-approach-wool-carpets)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/boosting-returns-kiwi-wool-growers)

### Support Farm Environment Plans administered by regional councils and targeted at a catchment level, and adopt standardised farm level reporting.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Largely delivered**  
`primary-nzf-catchment-farm-plans-reporting`

> Catchment-level farm plans -> $36m over four years for catchment group support announced June 2024

Catchment-level support and standardised measurement were delivered, but certification was shifted partly away from regional councils rather than administered by them.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/nzfirst/pages/4462/attachments/original/1700784896/National___NZF_Coalition_Agreement_signed_-_24_Nov_2023.pdf) - National-NZ First Coalition Agreement 2023
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/36-million-commitment-local-catchment-groups)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/government-delivers-standardised-emissions-measurement-tool)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/creating-certainty-rural-catchment-groups)

### Cut red tape and regulatory blocks on irrigation, water storage, managed aquifer recharge and flood protection schemes.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`primary-nzf-cut-rural-red-tape`

> Cut rural red tape -> 12 national direction instruments amended and 4 new ones proposed from May 2025; RMA replacement bills still in Parliament in Aug 2026

A large deregulation programme is well advanced but the central resource management replacement was still before Parliament as at August 2026.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/nzfirst/pages/4462/attachments/original/1700784896/National___NZF_Coalition_Agreement_signed_-_24_Nov_2023.pdf) - National-NZ First Coalition Agreement 2023
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/consultation-opens-sweeping-overhaul-primary-sector-regulations)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/updated-national-direction-natural-hazards-and-highly-productive-land)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/select-committee-report-natural-environment-bill-and-planning-bill-welcomed)

### Review the suitability of the systems dealing with by-catch within the Quota Management System, while retaining the QMS itself.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Largely delivered**  
`primary-nzf-bycatch-review`

> Review bycatch systems -> consultation drew over 22,000 submissions; retention ban on 19 reef fish species kept, 19 March 2026

The bycatch review the manifesto promised was carried out with public consultation and produced ministerial decisions. Parts of the package remain undecided.

- Promise (manifesto): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/nzfirst/pages/4158/attachments/original/1696631089/2023-New-Zealand-First-Policy-Manifesto-1.pdf) - New Zealand First 2023 Policy Manifesto, fisheries

- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/changes-proposed-deemed-value-fish)

### Amend the National Environmental Standards for Plantation Forestry to place a duty on harvesters to contain and remove post-harvest slash.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`primary-nzf-forestry-slash`

The change is being carried inside a wider resource management rewrite still before Parliament rather than delivered as a standalone amendment.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/nzfirst/pages/4462/attachments/original/1700784896/National___NZF_Coalition_Agreement_signed_-_24_Nov_2023.pdf) - National-NZ First Coalition Agreement 2023
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/consultation-opens-sweeping-overhaul-primary-sector-regulations)

### Reverse the recent ban on live animal exports while ensuring the highest standards of animal welfare.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Partly delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`primary-national-reverse-live-export-ban`

> Reverse the live export ban -> legislation timetable slipped from early 2025; exports had not resumed by Aug 2026

Consultation and policy work proceeded. But the ban had not been lifted and the trade had not resumed nearly three years into the term.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/nzfirst/pages/4462/attachments/original/1700784896/National___NZF_Coalition_Agreement_signed_-_24_Nov_2023.pdf) - National-NZ First Coalition Agreement 2023 (the same commitment appears in the National-ACT agreement)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/government-progressing-commitment-reinstate-livestock-exports-sea)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/government-continues-work-reinstate-livestock-exports-sea)

### Improve farm plans so they are more cost-effective and pragmatic for farmers, tailored to the risks on the property and catchment rather than applied broadly.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`primary-national-freshwater-farm-plans`

> Cheaper, more pragmatic farm plans -> minimum farm size lifted from 20ha to 50ha, about 8,000 farms exempted

The system was legislatively rewritten along the lines promised, with a narrower scope and industry certification.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/actnz/mailings/6945/attachments/original/National_ACT_Agreement.pdf) - National-ACT Coalition Agreement 2023, improve Farm Environment Plans so they are more cost-effective and pragmatic for farmers
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/freshwater-farm-plan-systems-be-improved)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/farming-get-easier-improved-freshwater-farm-plans)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/southland-farmers-benefit-under-improved-farm-plan-system)

### Double the value of New Zealand's exports within a decade, with food and fibre exports carrying much of the growth.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`primary-national-double-exports`

> Double exports in a decade -> food and fibre exports forecast at $64.3b for the year to June 2026, projected $70.1b by 2030

Export values are rising and hitting records. But the forecast growth path to 2030 is far short of doubling, and the ten-year period runs past the current term.

- Promise (manifesto): [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/food-and-fibre-exports-set-hit-record-643-billion) - National 2023 commitment to double the value of New Zealand's exports within a decade, as restated by the Government at Fieldays 2026
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/food-and-fibre-exports-set-hit-record-643-billion)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/food-and-fibre-exports-set-reach-record-62-billion)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/new-zealand-secures-landmark-free-trade-agreement-india)

### Double the cap on Recognised Seasonal Employer workers to give horticulture and viticulture a bigger seasonal workforce.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Partly delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`primary-national-rse-cap`

> Double the RSE cap -> about 17,000 RSE workers a year as at July 2026, scheme changes staged 2027-2029

The scheme was reviewed and reworked but the published intake remains at about 17,000 a year, nowhere near double.

- Promise (manifesto): [rnz.co.nz](https://www.rnz.co.nz/programmes/news-extras/story/2018902030/election-2023-rnz-s-guide-to-party-policy) - National 2023 horticulture policy, as summarised in RNZ's guide to party policy
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/simpler-clearer-more-effective-rse-scheme)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/new-zealand-horticulture-sector-set-reach-record-high)

## Water & Local Government

_After the 2016 Havelock North campylobacter outbreak both governments accepted that New Zealand's council-owned water networks needed tens of billions of dollars of investment, but they disagreed fundamentally on who should own and run them. Labour legislating large centralised entities, the 2023 coalition repealing that model and replacing it with council-controlled organisations, rates restraint and city and regional deals._

### Establish an independent national drinking water regulator with oversight of drinking water, wastewater and stormwater networks.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered**  
`water-labour-drinking-water-regulator`

> New water regulator promised -> Taumata Arowai established 1 March 2021

The regulator was legislated, established and operational within the term, and has been retained by the successor government.

- Promise (manifesto): [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taumata_Arowai) - Labour commitment to a dedicated drinking water regulator following the 2017 Havelock North Inquiry
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taumata_Arowai)
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_Waters_reform_programme)

### Move drinking water, wastewater and stormwater services from 67 councils into four large publicly owned water services entities, operating from 1 July 2024.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`water-labour-three-waters-entities`

> 4 entities operating by 1 July 2024 -> rescoped to 10 entities from 1 July 2026, none ever operational

The legislation was passed, but the promised structural change was rescoped and delayed during the term, and no entity was operating at the October 2023 handover.

- Promise (manifesto): [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_Waters_reform_programme) - Labour 2020 term commitment to the Three Waters reform programme (Cabinet decisions announced October 2021)
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_Waters_reform_programme)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/labour%E2%80%99s-three-waters-legislation-repealed)

### Introduce a royalty on commercial users of water, including irrigators and water bottlers.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Superseded**  
`water-labour-water-royalty`

> Royalty on commercial water use -> dropped in coalition negotiation; no charge legislated by Oct 2023

The policy was dropped in the 2017 coalition negotiation with NZ First, before the government took office.

- Promise (manifesto): [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sixth_Labour_Government_of_New_Zealand) - Labour 2017 campaign policy, royalty on commercial water use
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sixth_Labour_Government_of_New_Zealand)
- Evidence: [nzherald.co.nz](https://www.nzherald.co.nz/nz/labour-nz-first-coalition-at-a-glance/OGW5YCYCI4D4AHS6YIS64UKZNA/)

### Introduce a tax on exports of bottled water.

**NZ First** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Not delivered**  
`water-nzfirst-bottled-water-tax`

> Bottled water export tax -> never introduced

This was an explicit coalition agreement commitment with a specific tax base, and it was never legislated over two full terms.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [nzherald.co.nz](https://www.nzherald.co.nz/nz/labour-nz-first-coalition-at-a-glance/OGW5YCYCI4D4AHS6YIS64UKZNA/) - Labour-NZ First Coalition Agreement, 24 October 2017 (tax section)
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sixth_Labour_Government_of_New_Zealand)

### Share a portion of the GST collected on new residential construction with local councils, so councils benefit financially from consenting new housing.

**ACT** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`water-act-gst-sharing`

> Share of GST on new builds -> $400m Incentives for Growth Fund over 4 years, first payments 1 April 2027

The underlying objective, paying councils for enabling housing growth, was delivered. But it came through a capped appropriation rather than the promised share of GST revenue, and payments start after the term ends.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [nzherald.co.nz](https://www.nzherald.co.nz/nz/new-government-coalition-details-at-a-glance-what-you-need-to-know/FBTX3E6HXREFXDTOA6AA5K7IUQ/) - National-ACT coalition agreement 2023, share a portion of GST on new residential construction with councils
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/incentivising-councils-go-housing-growth)
- Evidence: [hud.govt.nz](https://www.hud.govt.nz/our-work/going-for-housing-growth-programme)

### Replace the centralised Three Waters entity model with locally owned and controlled water services, allowing councils to voluntarily form water services groupings.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`water-nzfirst-council-ownership`

> Voluntary council-owned model -> 44 councils chose CCOs, 23 in-house; 19 water CCOs established

Council ownership was retained, and the voluntary grouping model was legislated and is operating.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sixth_National_Government_of_New_Zealand) - National-NZ First coalition agreement 2023 (water commitments, also in the National-ACT agreement)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/local-water-done-well-victory-ratepayers)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/major-milestone-reached-local-water-done-well)

### Repeal Labour's Three Waters legislation in the Government's first 100 days and restore council ownership and control of water assets.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`water-national-repeal-three-waters`

> Repeal within 100 days -> Water Services Acts Repeal Act passed 14 February 2024

The repeal was passed inside the promised 100-day window, exactly as committed.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/government-repeal-three-waters-legislation) - Coalition Government 100-day plan / 2023 coalition agreements, repeal Three Waters
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/labour%E2%80%99s-three-waters-legislation-repealed)
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Local_Water_Done_Well)

### Pass transitional legislation by mid-2024 and a second, substantive Local Water Done Well Act by mid-2025. Require every council to produce a Water Services Delivery Plan showing financially sustainable, ring-fenced water services.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Largely delivered**  
`water-national-local-water-done-well`

> Second Act by mid-2025 -> passed 19 August 2025; all mainland councils had approved plans by 1 July 2026

The framework, plans and approvals were all delivered, but both bills passed later than the dates the Government set itself.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/delivering-local-water-done-well) - Local Water Done Well implementation plan, announced 5 April 2024
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Local_Water_Done_Well)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/local-water-done-well-victory-ratepayers)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/major-milestone-reached-local-water-done-well)

### Reach an agreement with Auckland Council that keeps Watercare in local ownership while separating its balance sheet, so Auckland can fund water infrastructure without steep water price rises.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`water-national-watercare-auckland`

> Avoid Watercare's proposed price path -> 25.8% increases avoided; approx $899m saving over four years

The agreement was reached, endorsed by the council, confirmed by the ratings agency and legislated within the first year of the term.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/agreement-delivers-local-water-done-well-auckland) - Local Water Done Well. Auckland financial arrangement
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/major-milestone-reached-local-water-done-well)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/unlocking-local-water-done-well-new-water-service-delivery-models)

### Establish economic regulation of water services by the Commerce Commission by late 2025 and ring-fence water revenue. Let the Local Government Funding Agency lend to water CCOs at up to 500 per cent of operating revenue.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Largely delivered**  
`water-national-economic-regulation`

> LGFA lending to water CCOs -> up to 500% of operating revenue, approx double council limits

Borrowing capacity and ring-fencing were delivered on time and the Commerce Commission regime is legislated. But economic regulation is being introduced in stages rather than fully operating from late 2025.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/unlocking-local-water-done-well-new-water-service-delivery-models) - Local Water Done Well, new water service delivery models, announced 8 August 2024
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Local_Water_Done_Well)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/local-water-done-well-victory-ratepayers)

### Set national wastewater environmental performance standards to cut the cost and delay of consenting wastewater treatment plants.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`water-national-wastewater-standards`

> National wastewater standards -> in force Dec 2025, covering 330+ plants, up to $830m savings over 35 years

The standards were consulted on, finalised and in force within the term.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/new-wastewater-standards-win-ratepayers) - Local Water Done Well, national wastewater environmental performance standards
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/new-wastewater-standards-win-ratepayers)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/major-milestone-reached-local-water-done-well)

### Refocus councils on core services by removing the four wellbeings from the purpose provisions of the Local Government Act. Introduce published performance benchmarks for councils.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **In progress**  
`water-national-back-to-basics`

> Four wellbeings removed from the Local Government Act -> Bill still before Parliament at Aug 2026

The policy was announced and legislation introduced, but the Local Government (System Improvements) Bill had not passed as at August 2026 and the term is unfinished.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/back-basics-local-government) - 'Back to basics for local government' reform programme, announced 21 August 2024
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/council-voting-be-restricted-elected-members)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/simpler-more-cost-effective-local-government)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/councils-invited-fast-track-local-reform)

### Investigate and introduce a cap on council rates increases to stop spending on non-core projects.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **In progress**  
`water-national-rates-cap`

> Rates cap -> proposed 2-4% per capita a year, transition from 1 Jan 2027, full model 1 July 2029

Policy settings were announced and consulted on, but no cap was in force as at August 2026, with full operation scheduled for 2029.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/back-basics-local-government) - 'Back to basics for local government', exploring council revenue capping, announced 21 August 2024
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/getting-rates-under-control-ratepayers)

### Establish a City and Regional Deals framework, invite up to five regions to submit proposals, and finalise New Zealand's first Regional Deal in 2025.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`water-national-regional-deals`

> First deal in 2025 and up to 5 regions -> first deal signed 10 April 2026; 2 deals signed by Aug 2026

The framework was delivered on time and two deals were signed. But the first deal came roughly a year after the promised 2025 date, and fewer than five regions had deals by August 2026.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/regional-deals-framework-announced) - National-ACT coalition agreement 2023. City and Regional Deals; framework announced 22 August 2024
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/landmark-auckland-deal-unlock-city%E2%80%99s-potential)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/western-bay-plenty-deal-signed-unlock-long-term-growth)

## Public Service & Regulation

_Labour expanded the public service and public media and legislated a series of democratic process changes, while the National-led coalition set out to shrink the workforce, cut consultant spending, rewrite the Public Service Act and impose new statutory tests on regulation, with the headline regulatory law passed but the Public Service Amendment Bill still unfinished as at August 2026._

### Hold a referendum on legalising the personal use of cannabis at, or by, the 2020 general election.

**Greens** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered**  
`public-service-greens-cannabis-referendum`

> Referendum by the 2020 election -> held 17 Oct 2020, 51.17 per cent no to 48.83 per cent yes

The promise was to hold the referendum by the 2020 election, and it was held on time. The outcome was not part of the commitment.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_New_Zealand_cannabis_referendum) - Labour-Greens Confidence and Supply Agreement 2017
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_New_Zealand_cannabis_referendum)
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sixth_Labour_Government_of_New_Zealand)

### Eliminate the gender pay gap within the public sector.

**Greens** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`gapfill-greens-public-sector-gender-pay-gap`

> Eliminate the public sector gender pay gap -> median gap fell from 10.7 per cent (2018) to 5.8 per cent (June 2023); mean gap 7.1 per cent

The gap roughly halved over the term, but the agreement promised elimination and it was not eliminated.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [thestandard.org.nz](https://thestandard.org.nz/labour-green-confidence-and-supply-agreement-released/) - Labour-Green Confidence and Supply Agreement 2017, policy goal 12, eliminate the gender pay gap within the public sector
- Evidence: [publicservice.govt.nz](https://www.publicservice.govt.nz/research-and-data/workforce-data-2023/workforce-data-2023-remunerationpay/workforce-data-2023-pay-gaps)
- Evidence: [publicservice.govt.nz](https://www.publicservice.govt.nz/system/public-service-people/pay-gaps-and-pay-equity/kia-toipoto)

### Merge Radio New Zealand and Television New Zealand into one new public media entity, Aotearoa New Zealand Public Media. It would run as an autonomous Crown entity from 1 March 2023.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Abandoned**  
`public-service-labour-rnz-tvnz-merger`

> Merged entity from 1 March 2023 -> cancelled 8 Feb 2023 at a sunk cost of about $19.6m

The promising party cancelled the merger outright in February 2023, after the bill had already passed its first reading.

- Promise (manifesto): [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aotearoa_New_Zealand_Public_Media) - Labour 2020 term commitment to a new public media entity, following the 2019 advisory group process and Cabinet consideration in January 2020
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aotearoa_New_Zealand_Public_Media)
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sixth_Labour_Government_of_New_Zealand)

### Ban foreign donations to political parties and candidates above a nominal threshold.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered**  
`public-service-labour-foreign-donations`

> Foreign donation cap cut to $50

The ban was legislated and in force well before the end of the term.

- Promise (manifesto): [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sixth_Labour_Government_of_New_Zealand) - Labour 2017-2020 term commitment to tighten electoral donation rules against foreign influence
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sixth_Labour_Government_of_New_Zealand)

### Restore the right to vote for prisoners serving sentences of less than three years, reversing the 2010 blanket ban.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered**  
`public-service-labour-prisoner-voting`

> Voting restored for sentences under 3 years; ban retained for 3 years and over

The promised partial restoration was legislated within the term. Lifting the wider ban was never promised.

- Promise (manifesto): [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sixth_Labour_Government_of_New_Zealand) - Labour 2020 term commitment to act on the Supreme Court's declaration of inconsistency and the Waitangi Tribunal's findings on the prisoner voting ban
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sixth_Labour_Government_of_New_Zealand)

### Provide multi-year contestable funding through NZ On Air to fund public interest journalism.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered**  
`gapfill-national-labour-public-interest-journalism-fund`

> $55m fund promised -> about $64m allocated, 73 projects and 219 roles funded by April 2023

The multi-year contestable fund was set up through NZ On Air and fully allocated within the term, as promised.

- Promise (manifesto): [s3.ap-southeast-2.amazonaws.com](https://s3.ap-southeast-2.amazonaws.com/nzdoctor.files/production/public/2020-10/Labour_Manifesto_2020.pdf) - Labour 2020 manifesto

### Protect the integrity of New Zealand elections and voters' access to the polls, including a review of election financing rules.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`gapfill-national-labour-electoral-finance-review`

> Review of financing rules -> disclosure changes legislated Dec 2022; Independent Electoral Review reported 30 Nov 2023, after the term

Targeted donation and loan disclosure changes were legislated. But the broader review of financing rules reported only after the term had ended, and produced no legislative change.

### Hold a binding referendum on overturning New Zealand's anti-smacking law.

**NZ First** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Superseded**  
`public-service-nzfirst-2017-smacking-referendum`

> Binding referendum promised -> dropped in the 2017 coalition negotiation, 0 held

The commitment was traded away in the 2017 coalition negotiation, before the government took office.

- Promise (manifesto): [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sixth_Labour_Government_of_New_Zealand) - NZ First 2017 election campaign commitment to binding referendums, including on the anti-smacking law
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sixth_Labour_Government_of_New_Zealand)

### Pass a Regulatory Standards Act setting principles of responsible regulation, so that all new and existing legislation is tested against those principles.

**ACT** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`public-service-act-regulatory-standards-act`

> Regulatory Standards Act -> passed 13 Nov 2025, in force 1 July 2026; about 23,000 submissions, 88 per cent opposed

The Act was passed and brought into force inside the term, with the review board established and operating.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [russellmcveagh.com](https://www.russellmcveagh.com/media/h1whr1gg/national-act-nz-first-coalition-government-policies-public-services.pdf) - National-ACT Coalition Agreement 2023

### Reduce Core Crown expenditure as a proportion of the overall economy.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`public-service-nzfirst-core-crown-expenditure`

> Public service FTEs 65,699 (Dec 2023 peak) -> 62,654 (Jun 2025) -> 64,535 (Mar 2026); target 55,000 by July 2029

Spending as a share of the economy was forecast to fall, but the reduction sits mostly beyond the end of this term. The public service workforce was also larger in March 2026 than a year earlier.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Not delivered** &middot; _contested call_

### Conduct a full-scale, wide-ranging, independent public inquiry into the Covid-19 pandemic response, covering matters the existing inquiry did not.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`public-service-nzfirst-covid-inquiry`

> Wider Covid-19 inquiry -> scope expanded 2 Feb 2024, phase two hearings from 7 July 2025, report 10 March 2026

A second, expanded inquiry phase covering the promised subject matter was established and reported inside the term.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/nzfirst/pages/4462/attachments/original/1700784896/National___NZF_Coalition_Agreement_signed_-_24_Nov_2023.pdf) - National-NZ First Coalition Agreement, signed 24 November 2023
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Commission_of_Inquiry_into_COVID-19_Lessons_Learned)
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_in_New_Zealand)
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sixth_National_Government_of_New_Zealand)

### Amend the Public Service Act 2020 to clarify the role of the public service, drive performance, and make it accountable for delivering the agenda of the government of the day.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`public-service-national-act-public-service-act-amendment`

> Amend the Public Service Act 2020 -> bill first reading 31 July 2025, not passed as at Aug 2026

Term unfinished: the bill was introduced and passed a first reading, but it had not been enacted with under three months of the term remaining.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [russellmcveagh.com](https://www.russellmcveagh.com/media/h1whr1gg/national-act-nz-first-coalition-government-policies-public-services.pdf) - National-ACT Coalition Agreement 2023
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/back-basics-public-service)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/public-service-get-back-basics-deliver-value)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/portfolio/nationalactnew-zealand-first-coalition-government-2023-2026/public-service)

### Cut public service spending on consultants and contractors by $400 million over two years to 2024/25.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`public-service-national-consultant-spending`

> $400m saved on consultants and contractors over 2 years -> about $800m by June 2025

The savings target was met and roughly doubled inside the promised period, on the Government's own published figures.

- Promise (manifesto): [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/cuts-consultant-contractor-spend-exceed-target) - National 2023 campaign commitment to cut back-office and contractor spending, given effect by the Government's savings target set in Budget 2024
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/cuts-consultant-contractor-spend-exceed-target)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/latest-data-shows-size-public-service-decreasing)
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/portfolio/nationalactnew-zealand-first-coalition-government-2023-2026/public-service)

### Reduce the size and number of government departments and back-office functions, protecting frontline staff such as teachers, doctors, nurses and police.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Partly delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`public-service-national-department-mergers`

> Back-office reduction promised -> 4 agencies merged into MCERT from July 2026; 8,700 further cuts scheduled to mid-2029, FTEs 64,535 as at March 2026

Real agency mergers and savings were achieved. But the public service was still larger in March 2026 than in mid-2025, and most of the promised reduction is scheduled for after this term.

- Promise (manifesto): [globalgovernmentforum.com](https://www.globalgovernmentforum.com/new-zealand-government-announces-plan-to-cut-nearly-9000-public-service-jobs/) - National 2023 campaign commitment to reduce back-office bureaucracy and the number of government agencies while protecting frontline services
- Evidence: [beehive.govt.nz](https://www.beehive.govt.nz/release/new-ministry-deliver-better-housing-transport-and-environmental-outcomes)
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_New_Zealand_budget)
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_in_New_Zealand)
- Evidence: [publicservice.govt.nz](https://www.publicservice.govt.nz/data/workforce-data/public-sector-composition/workforce-size)
