## Foreign Affairs & Defence

_Across both eras New Zealand governments promised more trade agreements and better-equipped armed forces: Labour concluded deals with the UK, EU and China and re-equipped the air force, while the National-led coalition signed an FTA with India, committed to lifting defence spending above 2 percent of GDP and stopped short of joining AUKUS Pillar Two._

### Negotiate and bring into force a comprehensive free trade agreement with the United Kingdom.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered**  
`foreign-labour-uk-fta`

> UK FTA -> in force 31 May 2023, tariffs removed on 99.5 percent of NZ exports

The agreement was negotiated, signed, ratified and in force before the end of the term.

- Promise (manifesto): [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand%E2%80%93United_Kingdom_Free_Trade_Agreement) - Labour-led Government trade agenda, conclude a comprehensive free trade agreement with the United Kingdom after Brexit
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand%E2%80%93United_Kingdom_Free_Trade_Agreement)
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_trade_agreements_of_New_Zealand)

### Conclude a comprehensive free trade agreement with the European Union.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Largely delivered**  
`foreign-labour-eu-fta`

> EU FTA -> signed July 2023, in force 1 May 2024

The agreement was negotiated and signed within the term. But entry into force and the resulting benefits fell after the handover, and agricultural access was narrower than sought.

- Promise (manifesto): [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_New_Zealand) - Labour-led Government trade agenda, conclude a high-quality free trade agreement with the European Union
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_New_Zealand)
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_trade_agreements_of_New_Zealand)

### Sign the Trans-Pacific Partnership only on terms that keep New Zealand's ability to ban overseas buyers from buying existing homes, and that limit investor-state dispute settlement.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Largely delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`foreign-labour-cptpp-renegotiation`

> CPTPP -> signed 8 Mar 2018, in force for NZ 30 Dec 2018, ISDS provisions suspended

The housing ban was kept and put in place before entry into force, and ISDS was suspended and further limited by side letters. But ISDS was suspended rather than removed, and the rest of the agreement carried over largely unchanged.

- Promise (manifesto): [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comprehensive_and_Progressive_Agreement_for_Trans-Pacific_Partnership) - Labour 2017 manifesto and campaign commitments on the Trans-Pacific Partnership, preserve the right to ban foreign purchases of existing homes and curb investor-state dispute settlement
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comprehensive_and_Progressive_Agreement_for_Trans-Pacific_Partnership)
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overseas_Investment_Amendment_Act_2018)

### Complete and sign an upgrade of the 2008 New Zealand-China free trade agreement to improve access for New Zealand exporters.

**Labour** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered**  
`foreign-labour-china-fta-upgrade`

> China FTA upgrade -> signed 26 Jan 2021; all dairy tariffs gone 1 Jan 2024

The upgrade was negotiated and signed within the term, and delivered the tariff outcomes sought.

- Promise (manifesto): [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China%E2%80%93New_Zealand_relations) - Labour-led Government trade agenda, complete the upgrade of the 2008 New Zealand-China free trade agreement
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China%E2%80%93New_Zealand_relations)
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_trade_agreements_of_New_Zealand)

### Re-examine the Defence procurement programme within the context of the 2016 Defence Capability Plan budget.

**NZ First** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Delivered**  
`foreign-nzfirst-defence-procurement-review`

> 2016 plan re-examined -> $20b Defence Capability Plan 2019, 4 P-8As and 5 C-130Js ordered

The procurement programme was reviewed and rebuilt into a published capability plan, and the two largest replacement purchases were committed inside the term.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [mcguinnessinstitute.org](https://www.mcguinnessinstitute.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/WP202007-202009-AnalysisOfThe2017Labour-FINAL.pdf) - Labour-NZ First Coalition Agreement 2017, item 27, re-examine the Defence procurement programme within the context of the 2016 Defence Capability Plan budget
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sixth_Labour_Government_of_New_Zealand)
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boeing_P-8_Poseidon)
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lockheed_Martin_C-130J_Super_Hercules)

### Work towards a free trade agreement with the Russia-Belarus-Kazakhstan Customs Union.

**NZ First** &middot; Labour 2017-2023 &middot; **Not delivered**  
`foreign-nzfirst-russia-customs-union-fta`

> FTA with Russia-Belarus-Kazakhstan promised -> 0 negotiating rounds, no agreement

Talks were never restarted, and no agreement or negotiating round resulted from the commitment.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [mcguinnessinstitute.org](https://www.mcguinnessinstitute.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/WP202007-202009-AnalysisOfThe2017Labour-FINAL.pdf) - Labour-NZ First Coalition Agreement 2017, item 60, work towards a Free Trade Agreement with the Russia-Belarus-Kazakhstan Customs Union
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_trade_agreements_of_New_Zealand)
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sixth_Labour_Government_of_New_Zealand)

### Increase defence spending as part of ACT's alternative budget, after deferring the planned increase in its revised fiscal plan of 21 September 2023.

**ACT** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **In progress**  
`foreign-act-defence-spending-boost`

Defence funding has risen sharply under the government ACT is part of. But the build-up runs beyond the current term, and the party's original commitment carried no firm target date.

- Promise (manifesto): [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_New_Zealand_general_election) - ACT 2023 election fiscal plan, proposed defence spending boosts, revised on 21 September 2023 to delay tax cuts and the defence increase after the pre-election fiscal update
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judith_Collins)
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_New_Zealand_budget)

### Progressively increase real defence spending to reach 2 percent of GDP by 2030.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Partly delivered**  
`foreign-nzfirst-defence-2-percent-gdp`

> 2 percent of GDP by 2030 promised -> about 1 percent at the start of the term, $12b four-year plan announced April 2025

Large funding increases and a formal path above 2 percent are in place. But spending had not reached 2 percent as at August 2026, and the official timetable runs past 2030.

- Promise (manifesto): [nzfirst.nz](https://www.nzfirst.nz/2023_policies) - New Zealand First 2023 Policy Manifesto, progressively increase real Defence spending to reach 2% of GDP by 2030
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand_Defence_Force)
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judith_Collins)
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_New_Zealand_budget)

### Establish a permanent, ring-fenced Defence Capital Fund within the Budget to fund defence capability and estate procurement.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Not delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`foreign-nzfirst-defence-capital-fund`

> Permanent ring-fenced Defence Capital Fund promised -> not established; funding via four-year plan and annual Budgets

Defence capital was funded generously, but through normal appropriations. The specific permanent ring-fenced fund promised was not established.

- Promise (manifesto): [nzfirst.nz](https://www.nzfirst.nz/2023_policies) - New Zealand First 2023 Policy Manifesto, establish a permanent, ring-fenced Defence Capital Fund as part of the Budget
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_New_Zealand_budget)
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand_Defence_Force)

### Instruct NZDF uniformed staff to deliver a tri-service Defence Command Paper setting out the long-term size, structure and equipment needs of the Defence Force.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Largely delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`foreign-nzfirst-defence-command-paper`

> Tri-service command paper promised -> Defence Capability Plan 2025 released 7 April 2025, $12b over four years

A long-term capability document covering all three services was delivered early in the term, though not in the specific form the manifesto described.

- Promise (manifesto): [nzfirst.nz](https://www.nzfirst.nz/2023_policies) - New Zealand First 2023 Policy Manifesto, instruct NZDF uniformed staff to deliver a tri-service Defence Command Paper setting long-term size, structure and equipment needs
- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand_Defence_Force)

- Evidence: [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_in_New_Zealand)

### Continue work towards a full free trade agreement with the United States.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Not delivered**  
`foreign-nzfirst-us-fta`

> US FTA sought -> no negotiations; 10 percent US tariff Apr 2025 rising to 15 percent Aug 2025

No FTA negotiations with the United States were begun. New US tariffs on New Zealand goods dominated the period instead.

- Promise (manifesto): [nzfirst.nz](https://www.nzfirst.nz/2023_policies) - New Zealand First 2023 Policy Manifesto, continue work towards a full free trade agreement with the United States

### Launch a Closer Commonwealth Economic Relations agreement with the United Kingdom, Australia, Canada, Singapore, Malaysia and Brunei, with India and other Commonwealth states invited as associates.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Not delivered**  
`foreign-nzfirst-commonwealth-economic-relations`

> 6-country Commonwealth agreement promised -> not launched

The proposed Commonwealth grouping was never launched. Trade liberalisation with those countries went through existing agreements instead.

- Promise (manifesto): [nzfirst.nz](https://www.nzfirst.nz/2023_policies) - New Zealand First 2023 Policy Manifesto, launch Closer Commonwealth Economic Relations with the UK, Australia, Canada, Singapore, Malaysia and Brunei

### Deepen the Pacific Reset into engagement with the wider Indo-Pacific, in recognition of the region's importance to New Zealand's security and wellbeing.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Largely delivered**  
`foreign-nzfirst-pacific-reset-indo-pacific`

Pacific and Indo-Pacific engagement clearly deepened in diplomacy, defence agreements and aid. But New Zealand's influence in the Pacific was visibly contested, and aid funding remained modest.

- Promise (manifesto): [nzfirst.nz](https://www.nzfirst.nz/2023_policies) - New Zealand First 2023 Policy Manifesto, deepen the Pacific Reset to the greater Indo-Pacific region

### Run a 'National Interest Test' before New Zealand accepts any agreement from the UN or its agencies that limits national decision-making. By 1 December 2023, reserve against the proposed amendments to the World Health Organization's International Health Regulations.

**NZ First** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered**  
`gapfill-national-nzf-un-national-interest-test`

> Reservation due by 1 Dec 2023 -> lodged; 2024 IHR amendments formally rejected 16 March 2026

The national interest test was created and applied to the WHO instruments named in the agreement. Both the reservation and the later rejection were lodged.

- Promise (coalition agreement): [assets.nationbuilder.com](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/nzfirst/pages/4462/attachments/original/1700784896/National___NZF_Coalition_Agreement_signed_-_24_Nov_2023.pdf) - National. NZ First Coalition Agreement, 24 November 2023 (Strengthening Democracy and Freedoms)
- Evidence: [health.govt.nz](https://www.health.govt.nz/strategies-initiatives/programmes-and-initiatives/emergency-management/pandemics/strengthening-global-pandemic-prevention-preparedness-and-response/international-health-regulations)

### Prioritise free and fair trade agreements, including a free trade agreement with India.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **Delivered** &middot; _contested call_  
`foreign-national-india-trade-agreements`

> India FTA -> negotiations opened April 2025, signed April 2026, tariffs removed on 95 percent of NZ exports

An India FTA was launched and signed within the term, alongside two other new agreements, matching the commitment.

### Keep New Zealand's nuclear-free policy while exploring participation in the non-nuclear second pillar of AUKUS, covering advanced technology co-operation.

**National** &middot; National 2023-2026 &middot; **In progress** &middot; _contested call_  
`foreign-national-aukus-pillar-two`

> AUKUS Pillar Two -> exploratory consultations only, no participation decision by Aug 2026

Exploration and consultation continued through the term exactly as described. But no participation decision had been made as at August 2026, and the promise was to explore rather than to join.

- Promise (manifesto): [en.wikipedia.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christopher_Luxon) - National's stated position on AUKUS Pillar Two entering government, set out by Prime Minister Christopher Luxon during his December 2023 visit to Sydney
